Folded into [Phase 7](16-implementation-plan-ph7.md) Step 6.
- **LT-29 `TestEngineRun_HundredTargetsPerformance` deterministically over its 2m budget.** 2026-09-06: three back-to-back runs all landed at **2m13.00s** (byte-identical to the millisecond — not machine-load jitter; stopping every other container changed nothing) against the doc03 Phase 1b `<2m` metric. The test pins `--rate-limit 50`; its own doc comment computes a ~99s rate-limiter floor for misconfig's ~50 requests/target × 100 targets, leaving only ~21s for all engine overhead, and the real overhead is now ~34s. Cause is almost certainly misconfig's rule set having grown since doc03 was written (more requests/target → more limiter pacing), not an engine regression — `git log` shows no perf-relevant `pkg/scanner` change, and the test imports only `pkg/scanner`. Options: re-baseline the budget to ~2m30s with a comment explaining the floor math; or measure CPU-time / a rate-limit-normalized figure instead of raw wall-clock; or trim/parallelize misconfig's per-target request fan-out. Not blocking — it's a stale metric, and CI's default `go test ./...` doesn't run the `integration`-tagged perf test.
- **✅ done 2026-09-03 — CI coverage gate 79.0% → 77.0% → 80.0%.** Real repo-wide coverage had quietly drifted to ~77% across several Phase 6 commits, uncaught because `go test -race` was failing first on unrelated network-flakiness (later steps don't run after an earlier failure). Gate temporarily dropped to 77.0% once the masking was fixed, then closed back to a real 83.5% with new unit tests; gate raised to 80.0% (margin below the real number, not pinned to it). **Reusable hazard found**: this dev machine has real `subfinder`/`tlsx` binaries + a synced corpus at `os.UserConfigDir()`, so a naive "happy path against a local httptest target" test silently shelled out to them / loaded the full corpus — fixed via `t.Setenv("PATH", ""); t.Setenv("HOME", t.TempDir()); t.Setenv("XDG_CONFIG_HOME", "")` (`isolateFromInstalledReconBinaries` in `cmd/hackerfive` + `pkg/mcpserver` test helpers). Same bug class as the WHOIS/ASN loopback fix (doc15 addendum 7).

## Items resolved 2026-09-11 through 2026-09-24 (archived 2026-09-24)

Full original write-ups for everything trimmed out of follow-up.md in the 2026-09-24 pass, grouped under the live-testing round each item was originally filed against.

### From "Live Testing — ALSCO / Secure Gateway sandboxes (2026-09-07)"

- **LT-87 (partially fixed 2026-09-11 — see below; WAF-detect fact, 403-is-signal retry, and `uploadbypass` still open)** — no WAF-fingerprint step, no payload-mutation-on-403 retry, no upload-bypass detector, and — the base gap — no native `sqli`/`xss`/`lfi` detector at all, those classes covered solely by generic corpus templates. **Fix:** (1) WAF-detect recon signal; (2) retry a blocked payload with a mutation/encoding set and flag any bypass; (3) native `sqli`/`xss`/`lfi` detectors; (4) an `uploadbypass` detector. → [Phase 9](18-implementation-plan-ph9.md) Step 4, sequenced last (wants Phase 8's param surface first). A docs/22 target-selection caveat is already applied. **Applied (sub-item 3, sqli only — xss/lfi still open):** a first-party `pkg/detectors/sqli` package (error-based + boolean-based-blind + time-based-blind, each a small fixed payload set appended to a param's existing value, never an unbounded fuzzing engine) closes the "no native sqli detector at all" gap. A new `recon.SuggestSQLiTargets` mines the same ID-/value-shaped query-param surface `idorCandidatesAndSeeds` already mines for IDOR (an ID-named key with an ID-shaped value, or LT-83's unnamed-numeric-key-varying-across-≥2-observations signal), grouped by path; `registry.resolveEndpointFacts` fans out one `sqli` leaf per candidate path (`leaf.SQLiPath`/`SQLiParams`, mirroring LT-91's idor fan-out), `planexec.applyLeafReconFields` copies them into a blank `scanner.Config`, and `pkg/scanner/engine.go` dispatches `sqli.Detector.Run` (new `--detector sqli` value, `--sqli-path`/`--sqli-param` CLI flags for a manual run). Each technique is deliberately conservative to hold the <5% FP target: error-based requires a DBMS-specific error signature present in the payload response but *absent* from the baseline (never matched if the baseline already carries one); boolean-based requires the tautology response to match baseline *and* the negation to diverge (a param with no real effect produces true==false==baseline and is correctly never flagged); time-based requires the delay to repeat on an independent confirm request and skips entirely when the baseline itself is already slow. **Known, deliberately deferred gaps** (same precedent as ssrf's body-params having no CLI flag): no webui Launch-page tab, no MCP tool wiring, and no `fieldsuggest.Deterministic` auto-fill for the bare `scan --recon-file` CLI path — the recon→decisionengine→planexec pipeline (Plan Preview, MCP agent) is fully wired and is the primary path this was built for. Tests: `pkg/recon/suggest_test.go` (`TestSuggestSQLiTargets*`), `tests/unit/detector_sqli_test.go` (error/boolean hit + 4 decoy/no-finding cases, multi-param independence, auth-header propagation), `pkg/detectors/sqli/timebased_test.go` (internal white-box, test-scale sleep durations: hit+repeat-confirmed, delay-doesn't-repeat, slow-baseline-skipped).

### From "Live Testing — crAPI actionable-findings prep (2026-09-08)"

- **LT-132 ✅ done 2026-09-12 (DELETE only — PUT/PATCH deferred, see below)** — no authbypass/idor check ever probed a non-GET method, so crAPI's real `DELETE /workshop/api/merchant/video/delete/{id}` BFLA (delete another user's video) was structurally unreachable. **Applied:** a new first-party `pkg/detectors/mutatebfla` package — the second detector in the project (after businesslogic) to perform a genuinely mutating request, and the first that is *intentionally* destructive when it confirms a real bug (a real DELETE against the owner's own resource, fired with the other account's token). Safety-first design, matching the concern named when this item was opened:
  - the resource under test is never enumerated/guessed — the caller supplies a concrete `--mutatebfla-delete-path` (real ID already substituted) that the operator has independently confirmed the owner account owns; there is no idor-style ID-range mode here;
  - the detector re-confirms that ownership itself before ever mutating anything: a baseline GET as the owner account must return `200` and contain an explicit, caller-supplied `--mutatebfla-marker` substring (typically the resource's own ID) — a `Target` with no marker, or whose baseline doesn't contain it, is skipped with **zero requests sent beyond the baseline GET**, never a guess;
  - the finding is only ever recorded from a **follow-up GET as the owner**, after the other account's DELETE, showing the marker genuinely gone — never inferred from the DELETE response's own status code, which a real backend can misreport (a `200`/`204` that didn't actually delete anything);
  - gated behind its own flag, `--allow-mutating-bfla` — a *third*, independently-scoped exception to the read/enumerate-only default, deliberately never folded into `--allow-writes` (businesslogic) or `--auto-provision-account` (pkg/provision), per CLAUDE.md's "any new mutating capability needs its own equally-scoped flag" rule. Absent, the detector is skipped with a once-per-scan stderr warning, same treatment as `--allow-writes`.
  Requires both `--auth-token` (owner) and `--other-auth-token` (other) — there is no single-account mode, since a one-token run has no way to know whose resource is under test. `--mutatebfla-verify-path` defaults to `--mutatebfla-delete-path` (the common RESTful case: same path, GET vs DELETE) but can point at a different endpoint (e.g. a list endpoint) for a target that splits them the way crAPI's own `.../video/delete/{id}` does. **Known, deliberately deferred gaps:** (1) **PUT/PATCH** are not built — same harness, just an inverted marker polarity (absent → present) plus a request body, explicitly parked as a near-identical follow-up rather than folded in here; (2) **no recon/decisionengine/planexec/webui/MCP auto-derivation** — unlike `EndpointTemplate`/`SQLiPath`, recon has no way to determine which concrete ID belongs to which account, so this is CLI-flag-driven only for now, by design, not an oversight. Tests: `tests/unit/detector_mutatebfla_test.go` (real-deletion hit, properly-protected no-finding, the misleading-status-code decoy, the unconfirmed-baseline-never-mutates safety test, the gate-blocks-every-request test, missing-token no-op, a crAPI-shaped split verify-URL case, auth-token propagation, and Finding.ID sanitization).
- LT-135 ✅ done 2026-09-10 — `businesslogic`'s coupon checks were previously crAPI-only in practice: hardcoded field/response-field names on top of the already-overridable paths. `responseGrantedAmount` generalized to a bounded recursive scan for any numeric field within [90%,150%] of the injected amount; new spec-derived `ReconResult.CouponEndpoint`/`CouponFact` (schema v1.12) extracts both the mint+apply paths *and* their real field names from an OpenAPI spec (no path-guess fallback — recon must stay read-only and a coupon-mint endpoint is POST-only/mutating). `--allow-writes` + a spec-carrying `--recon-file` now needs zero manual coupon flags. Tests: `pkg/recon/specwalk_test.go`, `tests/unit/detector_businesslogic_test.go`, `pkg/registry/decisionengine_test.go`.

### From "Live Testing — yosmart.com VDP-report review (2026-09-13)"

- **LT-156 ✅ done 2026-09-13** — neither of two real, common misconfiguration classes the report's own findings 1 and 5 hit were covered by `misconfig`'s `ExposedPaths` list or any other check: a Prometheus `/metrics` scrape endpoint, and CWE-601 host-header-poisoned redirects. **Fix:** (1) added `{Path: "/metrics", Keywords: []string{"# HELP ", "# TYPE "}, Severity: "high"}` to `pkg/detectors/misconfig/rules.go`'s `ExposedPaths` — Prometheus's exposition-format markers are effectively zero-FP. (2) new `checkHostHeaderRedirect` (`pkg/detectors/misconfig/detector.go`): spoofs `req.Host` to a `.invalid` canary on a GET `/`, using a new `httpclient.Client.DoNoRedirect` (shares the existing client's Transport, never follows a redirect — so a reflected Location is inspected without this detector ever actually connecting to whatever it points at) and flags a `misconfig-host-header-reflected-redirect` (low/high) finding when the response's `Location` echoes the spoofed Host back. Found live that the vulnerable behavior was specifically on `www.yosmart.com`'s plain-HTTP listener, not its (correctly SNI/Host-matched, 404-on-mismatch) HTTPS listener — a common real-world shape, since the plain-HTTP listener's only usual job is the http→https upgrade redirect and is often configured more loosely. So `checkHostHeaderRedirect` tries the target's own scheme first, then falls back to the plain-http variant (`httpDowngrade`) if that found nothing. Tests: `TestMisconfigExposedPath_PrometheusMetrics_Hit`, `TestMisconfigHostHeaderRedirect_Reflected`, `TestMisconfigHostHeaderRedirect_FixedTarget_NoFinding`, `TestHttpDowngrade`, `TestClient_DoNoRedirect_NeverFollows`. Both new checks confirmed live: re-scanning `api.yosmart.com`/`www.yosmart.com` after the fix produced `misconfig-exposed-path-metrics` (high) and `misconfig-host-header-reflected-redirect` (low, `target: http://www.yosmart.com` — the fallback path, exactly as expected).

### From "Live Testing — `hackerfive agent` reasoning verification, isolated lab targets (2026-09-20, post-LT-167)"

- **LT-172 (IMPLEMENTED + live-verified 2026-09-20/21, branch `fix/agent-orchestrator-lt168-171-172`) — the LLM adds latency, not judgement, for the cheap deterministic part of the tree.** vAPI turns 1-6 (five single-template leaves plus the `netservice` port check) and DVWA turns 1-8 (all single-template leaves) each finished their scan in 0.1-1.6s with 0 findings, each preceded by a 1-4 min `NextAction` round trip. DVWA's model walked leaves in literal numeric order (leaf-1 … leaf-8; rationales on turns 4-8: "Continue scanning the next pending high-confidence Apache CVE check…"). The one broad, high-yield leaf — generic `misconfig`, which produced *all* of DVWA's 13 and vAPI's 10 findings — was picked **9th** and **7th**. Tool diversity was zero on DVWA (9/9 `scan.leaf`; no `recon.refresh`/`registry.lookup`/`triage.rank`). **Fix:** a deterministic *fast lane* — dispatch pending leaves in a yield/cost priority order (capability leaves before single-template leaves, then confidence) with no LLM call, and call `NextAction` only at genuine decision points: fast lane drained, a finding that warrants follow-up, an `unresolved` leaf, thin recon. This is the "LLM only at the few genuine gaps" rule from [CLAUDE.md](../CLAUDE.md) and answers LT-165 item 2's open question ("skip `NextAction` when one viable leaf — needs eval data") with data: on vAPI+DVWA 14 of 17 dispatched turns were exactly this shape. Expected effect: vAPI 14 min → ~3, DVWA bounded by scan time not LLM latency. Pairs with LT-171 (what the model sees at the points where it *is* called). **Shipped:** `orchestrator.Config.FastLane` (CLI `--fast-lane`, default true; `--fast-lane=false` restores one-model-call-per-turn; the Web UI agent path sets it on). A leaf is fast-lane when it is pending with a detector and is either a raw template-ID leaf (`registry.LeafClass == "templates"`) or a parameter-free sweep (`misconfig`/`netservice`/`tls`); those run in `Priority` order (the decision engine's own `leafPriority`) with no `NextAction` call, each leaf at most once, recorded as `TurnRecord.Auto` turns and counted in the new `Result.FastLaneTurns` (they count toward neither `MaxIterations` nor the `MinIterations` floor — the floor stays about the model's own turns, or a drained fast lane would satisfy it instantly and re-open LT-162's early-stop). idor/authbypass/ssrf/sqli/businesslogic leaves, `unresolved` leaves, recon.refresh, triage and stop remain model decisions. A cancelled context is checked each fast-lane iteration. **Live, port-pinned scope (the LT-168 form), same model, one target at a time:** DVWA — before: 9 model turns, hit the 20-min cap, exit 0 with 4 fixture prefixes; after: **0 model turns, 11 fast-lane turns, $0, exit 0, all 11 leaves done, same 4 prefixes** (1001s, of which 16 min is the one broad `misconfig` scan — see LT-179). vAPI — before: 8 turns, exit 1 after two 4-min stalls, ~14 min; after: **0 model turns, 16 fast-lane turns, exit 0, 133s, 5/5 prefixes**. Juice Shop — 1 leaf, fast lane, 0 model turns, 2/5 (unchanged; the 3 misses are the stale-fixture / probe items already logged). **Consequence worth knowing:** on DVWA/vAPI/Juice every leaf is parameter-free, so `hackerfive agent` there is now fully deterministic and *no longer exercises the LLM at all* — these labs stopped being an agent-reasoning test; crAPI (endpoint-specific idor leaves) is the one that is. Tests: `TestIsFastLaneLeaf`, `TestNextFastLaneLeaf_HighestPriorityFirstThenTreeOrder_SkippingTried`, `TestHasActionableLeavesExcept_IgnoresTriedPendingLeaves`, `TestRun_FastLane_RunsParameterFreeLeafWithoutAskingTheModel` (0 `NextAction` calls), `TestRun_FastLaneOff_StillAsksTheModelForTheSameLeaf`, `TestRun_FastLane_CancelledContextEndsTheRunWithAnError`. **Not done:** the "a finding that warrants follow-up" decision point (a fast-lane finding never pauses the lane; the model sees it only when the lane drains) and a yield-ordered lane that puts the broad sweep ahead of single-template leaves regardless of `Priority`.
- **LT-171 (IMPLEMENTED + live-verified on crAPI 2026-09-21, branch `fix/agent-orchestrator-lt168-171-172`) — the orchestrator prompt is blind to the three things it would need to reason.** `buildOrchestratePrompt` (`pkg/llmfallback/orchestrate.go:170`) renders leaves as `id/target/detector/status/confidence/rationale`, tools, and history; a `scan.leaf` turn's result is only `"N finding(s), M log line(s)"`. So: (1) **no finding detail** — the model never learns a finding's ID/severity/target/whether it's new, so it can't chain (`.env` exposed → try the leaked creds; 401 on an API → `authbypass`; `/metrics` → infra recon) and `triage.rank` has nothing to be chosen *for*. **Live proof of the damage — crAPI turns 3-4:** both dispatches returned `"1 finding(s)"`; the finding was the identical `nuclei-openresty-detect-0` info hit re-firing (LT-166), not an IDOR result — yet turn 4's rationale states "leaf-24 and leaf-23 already confirmed IDOR on the /workshop/api/mechanic API", a false belief built from a bare count. (2) **No recon facts** — tech+versions, endpoint list, open ports, `AppSurface`/WAF/uniform-wall verdict never reach the model, so `recon.refresh` is blind and any `script.explore` script has nothing concrete to ground on (the design premise of that tool — JWT alg-confusion, ID-space decoding — needs the endpoint/token facts). (3) **Leaf detail is buried** — an `idor` leaf's `EndpointTemplate` is only visible if the rationale string happens to embed it. **Fix:** three compact, capped, read-only digest blocks in the prompt (findings digest with a per-turn "N new / M duplicate", recon digest, leaf key fields). Touches no invariant: the model still can't write a `Finding`/`Severity`/`Confidence` (doc02 Design Principle 5; LT-157's evidence-gate still applies). Also make `runScanLeafOnce`'s summary dedupe against findings already seen so a corpus re-fire reads "0 new". **Shipped:** `NextAction` now takes a `llmfallback.RunDigest`; `buildOrchestratePrompt` renders (1) each leaf's `endpoint=` template when it has one, (2) a `recon facts` block (technologies with host+confidence, app-surface verdict, API spec, endpoint counts, JS-secret count, uniform-wall hosts, up to 5 truncated recon warnings; built by `pkg/orchestrator/digest.go`, unions tech across `recon.refresh`), (3) a `findings confirmed so far (distinct)` block (id/type/severity/target, capped at 25, overflow counted), and (4) history that collapses `Auto` turns to a count so a long fast lane can't push the model's own decisions out of the 10-turn window. The system prompt now says the tree, recon facts and findings list are ground truth and that a count in a turn summary is never evidence of a specific finding. `scan.leaf` results now read `N new finding(s) [id severity target; …], M repeat(s) of already-recorded findings ignored, K log line(s)`, and a repeat is neither appended to `Result.Findings` nor re-streamed via `OnFinding` (this also removes LT-166's *symptom* from the reported output — the redundant re-scan itself is still open). All prompt text is lab-neutral. Every turn is also logged via `OnLog` with the model's rationale (`turn N: scan.leaf <id> (why: …) — <result>`; also LT-170 (b)). **Live, crAPI:** before — turn 4 claimed "leaf-24 and leaf-23 already confirmed IDOR" from a bare `1 finding(s)` that was a re-fired info detection. After — the model's turn 1 picked the `mechanic_report` idor leaf (9 real `idor-*` findings), turns 3-5 read `0 new finding(s), 1 repeat(s) of already-recorded findings ignored`, and its rationales now cite what is actually on the findings list ("the sibling …/mechanic_report endpoint already confirmed 9 high-severity IDORs" — true, and grounded; turn 5 misstates 9 as 8). Turn 2 chose an `authbypass` leaf that had no `protected_paths` and skipped, so the digest did not steer it away from a leaf recon had no candidate for (see LT-180). Tests: `TestBuildOrchestratePrompt_ShowsEndpointTemplateReconFactsAndFindings`, `…_EmptyDigestSaysSo`, `…_DigestSectionsAreBounded`, `…_AutoTurnsCollapseToACount`, `TestRun_ModelSeesReconDigest`, `TestReconDigest_*`, `TestScanLeafSummary`, `TestDispatchScanLeaf_RefiredFindingIsNotRecordedTwice`. **Not done:** a per-turn "new vs already-known" flag on each digest finding, an endpoint list in the recon digest, and any A/B measurement of whether the richer prompt changes leaf choice (one crAPI run is anecdote, not an eval).
- **LT-173 (✅ fixed 2026-09-21, branch `feat/llm-finding-capability-phase0`; original diagnosis below) — one LLM stall kills an otherwise healthy run; the decision call has no output cap.** vAPI: turn 9's `NextAction` timed out at 4m0s, retried once, timed out again → `Run` returned `orchestrator: NextAction: … context deadline exceeded`, exit=1, with 10 findings in hand and 13 leaves still pending (`triage.rank` had run once on turn 8). crAPI's turn 5 timed out the same way (4m0s). `chatRequest` (`pkg/llmfallback/client.go:214`) is `{model, messages, temperature}` only — **no `max_tokens`, no reasoning-effort control** on a ~150-token JSON decision, so a reasoning-capable model can burn minutes on hidden reasoning tokens (the likely cause of LT-165's 60-150s+ latency, not just prompt size). **Fix:** (a) pass `max_tokens` (~600) and, where the provider supports it (OpenRouter's `reasoning` object), low/disabled reasoning effort for the orchestrator's decision calls — verify per-model support before relying on it; (b) on a `NextAction` failure after its one retry, *degrade* rather than error-exit: fall through to LT-172's fast lane (or stop cleanly, exit 0, "LLM unavailable — N leaves left undispatched") and still run `triage.rank`. Matches the standing preference for logged-warning-over-blocking on unattended paths. **Shipped:** (a) `NextAction` runs under an output envelope — `max_tokens` 2048, OpenRouter `reasoning: {effort: "low"}` (frontier tier only; overridable with `HACKERFIVE_DECISION_REASONING_EFFORT`, empty = send no `reasoning` field), and a 120s per-attempt deadline instead of 240s (a stalled call now costs 4 min including the client's one retry, not 8). `max_tokens` is deliberately generous because on most providers it covers hidden reasoning *and* the answer (OpenRouter's own docs), so a tight cap on a reasoning model comes back empty with `finish_reason: "length"`; that case is now an error (`errTruncatedResponse`, cost still counted) rather than an empty decision. (b) After `MaxConsecutiveLLMFailures` (2) consecutive failed decision calls `Run` stops asking the model, runs whatever the fast lane can still do, and ends cleanly with `Result.Degraded` set (reason plus how many leaves were left because they need a decision) instead of returning an error with findings in hand; a cancelled context still errors, and one failure followed by a success resets the count. **Live probe** (`TestLive_DecisionEnvelope`, gated on `HACKERFIVE_LIVE_LLM=1`, 2-3 trials per arm so directional only): `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` mean 12.6s → 7.7s and ~40% cheaper, 2/2 valid decisions in both arms; `openai/gpt-5.6-luna` 3.3s → 2.7s, 3/3 valid in both. **Not reproduced:** the 4-minute stall itself — neither model stalled on this small prompt, so whether the cap *prevents* the stall is unproven; the degrade path is what guarantees a stall no longer kills a run. Tests: `TestCompleteWith_*`, `TestDecisionCallOpts_*`, `TestRun_ModelFailsRepeatedly_DegradesInsteadOfErroring`, `TestRun_Degraded_CountsLeavesThatNeededADecision`, `TestRun_SingleTransientModelFailure_IsRetriedNotDegraded`, `TestRun_NonConsecutiveModelFailuresDoNotDegrade`, `TestRun_ModelErrorWithCancelledContext_StillErrors`.
- **LT-168 (FIXED 2026-09-20, commit 1f36f2f; was a regression that blocked the eval harness) — a port-pinned scope entry silently disables recon Wave 2.** LT-167's fix made `Scope.Allowed` honor `host:port`; but `filterScope` (`pkg/recon/passive.go:339`) checks every Wave-1 candidate as `Allowed("https://" + bareHost)` — port 443 — so under `--scope localhost:3000` the seed host is rejected, `inScope` is empty, and `runWave2` returns at `active.go:79` (`len(inScopeHosts)==0`) before dnsx/naabu/httpx run at all. **No warning is emitted** (the recon result's `warnings` list only carries the LT-35/WHOIS lines); `hackerfive agent` then sees an empty tree and exits 0 (LT-170). Reproduced live: same target, `--scope localhost` → 4 tech facts, host found; `--scope localhost:3000` → 0 tech, 0 hosts, `out_of_scope: [localhost]`. **This is exactly what `tests/eval`'s `hostScopeEntry` (LT-167's own fix) now writes**, so `TestOrchestratorEvalHarness`/`TestAgentEvalHarness` run recon-blind for every non-default-port target (Juice :3000, vAPI :8000, crAPI :8888) — the 2026-09-20 baseline predates that change and can't be reproduced as documented. **Fix:** the seed target already passed the full-URL `Allowed(target)` check at `recon.go:445`, so the seed's own host:port should bypass `filterScope` and reach httpx (the `runWave2` doc comment already says "target's own host:port is always included"); add a `ReconResult` warning whenever Wave 2 is skipped for lack of in-scope hosts; and skip naabu's top-100 port sweep when the scope pins a port (it probes ports outside the pin). Regression test: recon against an `httptest` server on a non-default port with a port-pinned scope. **Fix as shipped:** `filterScope` now takes the seed hostname and neither drops it silently nor reports it out of scope (Run already authorized its exact host:port); `runWave2` probes just `host:port` with httpx when the seed is authorized only at its pinned port, skips dnsx and the naabu top-100 sweep for it (the pin does not authorize other ports), and emits a `wave2: --scope pins … (LT-168)` warning. Tests: `TestRun_PortPinnedScope_ProbesOnlyThePinnedOrigin` (httpx gets exactly `host:port`, no naabu, seed not in `out_of_scope`, warning present) and `TestRun_PortPinnedScope_OtherPortStaysOutOfScope` (the exception does not widen scope). Not fixed here: LT-169 (port-blind fact attribution) is what makes a *bare*-host scope report a neighbouring lab's stack; a pinned scope now avoids it for the seed because httpx sees only that origin.
- **LT-166 (still open — new evidence).** crAPI turns 3 and 4 each ran a 1m30s-1m46s scan for an `idor` leaf and returned only the redundant `nuclei-openresty-detect-0` — the corpus re-fire, not the leaf's own check — and 3 of the 12 raw crAPI findings are that one finding three times. See LT-171: this isn't only wasted time, it fed the model a false "IDOR confirmed" belief. **Partial mitigation 2026-09-21 (LT-171):** the orchestrator now drops a finding it has already recorded (same ID+type+target+description), so a re-fire no longer inflates `Result.Findings`, no longer re-streams, and reads as "repeat(s) ignored" to the model. The wasted work is unchanged: crAPI turns 3-5 each still re-ran the whole scan for a leaf that found nothing new.

- **LT-180 (the underlying gap is largely closed 2026-09-21; the leaf-runnability display is still open) — the model still picks leaves that cannot run.** crAPI turn 2 chose an `authbypass` leaf and the dispatch came back `skipped: … no --protected-paths given and recon/I4 found no usable candidate`: the leaf's own status is `pending`, so nothing in the tree or the LT-171 digest says it is blocked on a required field, and a model turn (plus the per-turn latency) was spent to learn that. **Fix:** surface runnability in the leaf line (`needs=protected_paths` / `blocked: <reason>`, from `fieldMissDetectors` + the leaf's own required-field state), and/or keep a leaf that skipped for a field miss out of the offered list for the rest of the run (the fast lane's `tried` set already does this for its own leaves). **Update 2026-09-21:** the authbypass leaf skipped for "no protected paths" was the same recon gap as LT-186: verified JS-bundle routes lost their status, so no 401/403 path existed. They now keep it (and `AuthRequired`), and on crAPI the JWT alg-none check is found with no model, with or without recon authentication. What remains of LT-180 is only the display of runnability in the leaf line.
- **LT-183 (open) — Phase 0's measurement exists but has produced no numbers yet.** (a) **[done 2026-09-21 — see the update at the end of this item] No baseline table:** the first run (`TestAblation`, crAPI, `HACKERFIVE_ABLATION_RUNS=3`, model pinned) has not been done; until it has, there is no `no-model` vs `fast-lane+model` comparison to build J1-J6 against, and a 1-run model row is an anecdote. (b) **Ground truth only for crAPI:** DVWA, vAPI and Juice Shop have no `known-vulns` fixture (their `OrchestratorScenario.KnownVulnsFile` is empty, so those labs grade on `expected-findings` alone and cannot show a model's contribution). They must be sourced from each lab's documented challenge list and live-verified, not written from memory. crAPI's own list omits mass assignment, NoSQL/SQL injection and OTP rate limiting (no finding class or ID exists to grade them) and its `baseline` values are the 2026-09-08 verdicts, several since addressed by LT-95/96/132 and not re-measured. (c) **`crapi-bfla-delete-video` is unreachable by any agent arm** (`mutatebfla` needs `--allow-mutating-bfla` and an operator-supplied, ownership-confirmed path), so it will read as a miss in every arm; either drop it from the agent comparison or give the agent a gated route. (d) **`TestOrchestratorEvalHarness` still carries its own copy of the JSONL parser** (`agentStreamEvent`/`orchestratorResult`); `grade.go`'s `ParseAgentStream` is the shared one — fold the old test onto it. (e) **Model drift:** the `.env` model is now `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`; LT-162/171/172's numbers were `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash`. Any comparison against those earlier tables is across models. (f) `Unlabeled` counts findings matching no expected prefix and no known vuln — a candidate-FP signal, not a measured FP rate; a real precision number needs each lab's *true* finding list, which nobody has. **Update 2026-09-21 — first crAPI baseline run** (3 arms x 3 runs, bundled templates, `--rate-limit 60`, `openai/gpt-5.6-luna`, 9/9 runs completed, table in doc94 "First baseline"): control 6 findings / 1 of 2 expected / 1 of 7 known, 22 s, $0; fast-lane+model and model-every-turn both 15 findings / 2 of 2 / 2 of 7, about $0.017-0.020 and 4 minutes. The model's whole contribution is dispatching one real `idor` leaf out of 12; it found nothing no leaf already covered. **(g) [done 2026-09-21 — see the second update below] Missing control:** a fourth arm, `no-model` + run every runnable leaf, is needed before crediting that to reasoning rather than to the fast lane's exclusion rule. **(h)** Five of seven known vulns were missed by every arm because no leaf (or no usable leaf field) exists — LT-184, LT-180, and the missing recon candidates. **Second update 2026-09-21 — the fourth arm (`no-model+all-leaves`: `--no-model --run-every-leaf`) was added and run** (3 runs, same settings): 15 findings / 2 of 2 / 2 of 7, 194 s (191-198), $0 — the same as both model arms on every stored measure, 20-25% faster, free. On crAPI the model's leaf choice adds nothing a run-everything policy does not; the value has to come from creating leaves (J1/J2). Caveat: 14 runnable leaves is a small tree; a large tree would make run-all expensive, so selection is unproven rather than useless. **(i) [done 2026-09-23] Finding IDs are not recorded:** `RunRecord` stores counts and known-vuln hits, so "same findings" across arms is inferred, not shown; store the sorted finding IDs per run and diff them in the summary. **Update 2026-09-23:** `FormatFindingDiff` (`tests/eval/grade.go`) unions each arm's `finding_ids` across its runs (a model-driven arm is not deterministic run to run, so a per-run diff would just show that noise) and diffs every arm pair per lab, printing "identical (N finding(s))" when a pair's sets match exactly or the IDs unique to each side when they do not; wired into `TestAblation`'s own log line right after the miss-attribution table. Unit-tested (`TestFormatFindingDiff_IdenticalAndDivergentArms`, `TestFormatFindingDiff_SkipsALabWithOnlyOneArm`); not yet re-run against a live lab, so LT-196's "model arm ties the deterministic one" claim still hasn't been seen as an actual ID diff, only inferred from equal counts — that happens on the LT-183(j) larger-tree run this now feeds into. **(j)** A larger-tree lab (Juice Shop or a real scope with several hundred leaves) is needed before concluding anything about selection at scale. **Update 2026-09-22 — item (b)'s premise is now stale for two of the three labs, not just Juice Shop: vAPI already had `KnownVulnsFile` wired (`vapi.json`, predates this update) and Juice Shop now does too.** `tests/fixtures/known-vulns/juiceshop.json` added: 4 entries, each live-verified 2026-09-22 both by hand (curl, two throwaway accounts via `POST /api/Users`) and via the real CLI, not written from memory — BOLA on `GET /rest/basket/{id}` (found, `idor-`), a JWT `alg:none` signature-verification bypass on the same endpoint (found, `authbypass-jwt-`; a first pass nearly mis-attributed this to `/rest/user/whoami`, which turned out to accept literally any input including no `Authorization` header at all and so proves nothing — dropped in favor of the genuinely-gated basket endpoint), search-based SQLi on `GET /rest/products/search?q=` (found, `sqli-error-q-`, live UNION-extracted real user email+password-hash rows), and the classic login SQLi bypass on `POST /rest/user/login` (real, confirmed by hand — logs in as `admin@juice-sh.op` — but **missed**: the `sqli` detector only appends a payload to a URL query parameter's existing value, and this bug lives in a JSON request-body field, the same class of capability gap LT-188(a) closed for `ssrf`, not yet built for `sqli`). `OrchestratorScenario`'s Juice Shop entry now carries `KnownVulnsFile` plus optional `JUICESHOP_OWNER_TOKEN`/`JUICESHOP_OTHER_TOKEN` env vars (mirroring vAPI's convention); full setup in docs/20-setup-testing-targets.md's Juice Shop section. Not done: an actual agent-arm run against Juice Shop with tokens supplied, to see whether recon reaches these two endpoints at `--recon-depth active` the way it needed `full` for crAPI's endpoint-driven leaves — left unmeasured rather than guessed. DVWA still has no `known-vulns` fixture. **Update 2026-09-22 — measured: it reaches neither depth, root cause found.** `hackerfive agent -t http://localhost:3000 --recon-depth active|full --auth-token <A> --other-auth-token <B>` (two fresh throwaway accounts, `--templates <empty>` to isolate from the nuclei corpus): at `active`, wave3 (crawl + JS-static analysis) never runs at all, same as every other lab; at `full`, wave3 runs (~15s, katana crawls 23 URLs including `main.js` and every lazy-loaded component chunk) but the tree still ends up with exactly one host-level `misconfig` leaf — 0 `idor`/`authbypass`/`sqli` leaves, so none of `juiceshop.json`'s 3 "found" vulnerabilities are reachable by any agent arm at either depth, even though the deterministic CLI finds all 3 by hand today. Root cause, found by fetching `main.js` directly and grepping it: Juice Shop's Angular bundle builds every REST call path as a **backtick template literal** — `` .get(`${this.hostServer}/rest/basket/${e}`) ``, `` `/rest/products` ``, `` `/rest/user/login` `` — never a plain double/single-quoted string. `pkg/recon/jsstatic.go`'s primary literal-extraction regex, `jsQuotedStringRe = regexp.MustCompile(`"([^"\n]{2,200})"|'([^'\n]{2,200})'`)` (jsstatic.go:101), matches only `"..."`/`'...'` and has no backtick alternative, so every one of these real, present-in-the-bundle route strings is invisible to it — not a crawl-reach problem (the bundle *is* fetched) and not an auth/scope problem, a literal-syntax gap. This is a different failure shape from crAPI's (LT-186, an alphabetical cap and `<placeholder>`-segment rejection on otherwise-matched quoted strings) and from vAPI's (LT-189, a docs page nothing links to) — worth its own item since template-literal path construction is the default idiom in modern TypeScript/Angular code, not a Juice-Shop-specific quirk. A fix is a plausible, bounded follow-on to LT-186's existing placeholder-handling work (add a backtick alternative to `jsQuotedStringRe` and decide how to treat a `${...}` interpolation inside one — likely the same `<placeholder>`-segment treatment LT-186 already built for JS-syntax placeholders) but is real, separate work, not done here — tracked as **LT-190**.
**LT-190 — built and live-verified 2026-09-22, on the user's explicit "yes, build it" — with a real detour worth recording.** The first attempt was exactly the plan above: add a backtick alternative to `jsQuotedStringRe`'s regex, normalize a resulting `${...}` interpolation to `<name>`/`{name}` the same way LT-186 already treats a literal `<orderId>` segment. Unit tests against small synthetic snippets passed immediately. The live re-check against Juice Shop's real `main.js` (not a synthetic snippet) still found nothing: a diagnostic pass (`extractJSEndpoints` run directly against the fetched bundle, outside the recon pipeline) showed `/rest/basket/${e}` simply never appeared among the regex's matches at all, even though the raw text is genuinely there at byte offset 541517. Traced by hand: a completely unrelated template literal ~256KB earlier in the same bundle — Angular Material's own internal CSS-in-JS calc() logic, `` `calc(${this._currentDirection===`rtl`?`-1`:`1`} * (${`${a+l}px`} + ...` `` — nests a template literal three levels deep inside its own `${...}`. A flat regex has no way to track that nesting (regular languages fundamentally can't recognize balanced/nested delimiters); the "capture up to the next backtick" alternative paired the *closing* backtick of an unrelated, much-earlier literal with the *opening* backtick of `/rest/basket/${e}`, silently consuming it as part of a bogus match — and once one pairing desyncs, every later backtick pairing in the file does too. Raising the regex's length cap didn't fix it (Go's RE2 caps `{m,n}` at 1000 as it is, and the desync's cause was nesting, not length); an escape-aware version of the same regex didn't fix it either, for the same reason.

**Fixed properly**, not patched around: `pkg/recon/jsstatic.go` now extracts backtick literals with a small depth-tracking scanner (`consumeBacktickLiteral`/`extractBacktickLiterals`) instead of a regex — a backslash escapes the next character (a real `` \` `` exists in this exact bundle too, an angularx-qrcode error message, live-verified separately), and `${` opens an interpolation whose own brace depth is tracked so a nested `` ` `` recurses into a real nested-literal parse instead of ending the outer one early. `jsQuotedStringRe` itself is back to its original two-alternative (double/single-quote only) form — untouched, no evidence it has the same problem, so it wasn't touched. `normalizeJSTemplateLiteral` emits the OpenAPI-style `{name}` spelling directly now (not `<name>` first): a second live-verification pass found Juice Shop's real SQLi endpoint declared as one combined literal, `` `${this.hostServer}/rest/products/search?q=${e}` `` — a query VALUE interpolation, not a whole path segment, which the existing `<name>`-then-convert pipeline can't reach since it only rewrites whole `"/"`-delimited segments. `normalizeJSTemplateLiteral` now splits off a `?`-prefixed query string and normalizes its `&`-separated `key=${...}` pairs the same way, when the value is a whole interpolation.

**Live-verified against the real target, both ways.** Standalone recon (`hackerfive recon --recon-depth full`, real container, real tokens): 71 real endpoints, including `/rest/basket/{param}` (+ `/checkout`, `/coupon/{param}`), `/rest/products/search?q={param}`, and `/rest/user/login`, versus 23 before (junk `.js`/`.css` crawl artifacts only, zero `/rest/*` routes). The real `hackerfive agent` (`--recon-depth full`, both throwaway tokens, `openai/gpt-6-luna`): recon built a real `idor` leaf from `/rest/basket/{id}`, the model dispatched it on turn 1, and it found **real `idor-1` through `idor-7` findings** — cross-account basket reads, exactly `juiceshop-bola-basket`'s known-vuln entry, through the actual orchestrated path end to end, not a hand-built `--endpoint`. The run did not finish inside a 10-minute manual check (Juice Shop's dev server answered a single idor sweep against one endpoint in several minutes, not seconds) before reaching the sqli-search or authbypass-jwt leaves; `tests/eval`'s own ablation harness already defaults to a 45-minute per-run timeout, which should clear that, but hasn't been run against this lab yet to confirm. `OrchestratorScenario`'s Juice Shop entry moved from `active` to `full` depth to match.

Tests: `TestNormalizeJSTemplateLiteral` (no-interpolation pass-through, leading host-variable strip, whole-segment and query-value placeholders, embedded/malformed rejection), `TestExtractBacktickLiterals_NestedTemplateLiteralDoesNotDesyncLaterOnes` (the actual bug, reproduced at unit-test scale: a nested literal followed by an ordinary one, both must be found), `TestExtractBacktickLiterals_EscapedBacktickDoesNotEndTheLiteral`, `TestExtractBacktickLiterals_UnterminatedLiteralIsSkippedNotHung`, `TestExtractJSEndpoints_QuerySuffixTemplateLiteral`, plus the backtick-shape tests from the first attempt (still valid against the new implementation). `go build`/`vet`/`vet -tags eval`/`golangci-lint`: clean; `go test ./... -race`: clean except the pre-existing, unrelated LT-181 `pkg/scriptexec` failure.

**LT-190's own follow-up run, 2026-09-22 — the 45-minute ablation timeout question, answered, plus two new stage-1 gaps found.** `tests/eval`'s `TestAblation` had never actually been run against Juice Shop (LT-190's own live-verification used a manual 10-minute check that didn't reach every leaf). First real run (`fast-lane+model`, 1 run, 45-min default): killed at the timeout with **0 model turns** — the stderr tail showed the misconfig leaf's progress line stuck at `56/58 template(s) completed` for many consecutive prints. Root cause: `OrchestratorScenario`'s Juice Shop entry was the one lab left with no `Templates` override, so it ran against the full synced corpus the same way vAPI and crAPI did before their own fixes (both those scenarios' comments already describe the identical `signal: killed`, 0-progress symptom). Fixed the same way: added `Templates: templatesDir()` (crAPI's own precedent — its comment notes `.nuclei-templates-cache` avoids something the CLI's own no-`--templates` default path does that a directory-scoped run skips; not fully root-caused here either, but empirically it worked). Re-run: finished in 2275s (~38 min, under the cap), `result=true`, 5 model turns, $0.0018 — `idor-1` through `idor-7` found via the fast lane exactly as LT-190's manual check showed.

Both remaining known vulnerabilities were lost at **stage 1 ("recon endpoint(s) observed, but no leaf of this class covers them")** — recon extraction (LT-190) is working; the registry's leaf-builder (`pkg/registry/decisionengine.go`'s `resolveEndpointFacts`) just never turns those observed endpoints into an `authbypass`/`sqli` leaf. Traced to source, not guessed:
- **`juiceshop-authbypass-jwt-alg-none`** (`/rest/basket/{id}`): `recon.SuggestAuthBypassPathsFromRecon` (`pkg/recon/suggest.go:486`) only accepts a JS-static/api-spec route with `AuthRequired` when the path has **no** `{` — the comment there says a `{param}` route is deliberately "left to the idor path." That's a real design choice, but it conflates two orthogonal checks that happen to share a URL: idor asks "does account B's token read account A's object," authbypass's alg:none check asks "is a forged/unsigned token accepted at all" — a route being ID-parameterized says nothing about whether the second question is redundant. As written, no `{id}`-shaped route can ever generate an authbypass candidate through this path, cutting off the single most common shape of real protected surface (a per-resource REST endpoint) from the JWT-forgery check entirely.
- **`juiceshop-sqli-product-search`** (`/rest/products/search?q={param}`): `recon.SuggestSQLiTargets` (`pkg/recon/suggest.go:875`) only accepts a query value that passes `isIDShaped(val)` — true for a concrete crawled value (`?q=42`), never for LT-190's own `{param}` placeholder spelling. The function has no path at all for "this route's query value is templated, not literal" the way `SuggestIDOREndpointCandidates` already does for path segments — so a JS-extracted, never-crawled-with-a-real-value search endpoint is structurally invisible to sqli leaf-building, independent of anything LT-190 fixed.

Neither is Juice-Shop-specific: any SPA that builds its routes as JS template literals (LT-190's whole premise — "the default idiom in modern TypeScript/Angular code") will hit both the same way. `docs/eval/results/ablation-20260922-{181531,190256}.jsonl` (gitignored) carry both runs' full records.
**LT-191 (built and live-verified 2026-09-22, on the user's explicit "fix the two leaf-builder gaps") — a live-status probe, not a design change to the `{`-exclusion.** Rather than loosening `SuggestAuthBypassPathsFromRecon`'s `{`-exclusion directly (the path considered risky above — it would also have to distinguish "idor's territory" from "authbypass's territory" for every future `{param}` route, not just this one), the fix sits one layer earlier: a new bounded recon pass, `probeTemplatedRouteAuthBoundary` (`pkg/recon/endpointprobe.go`), runs right after `runJSStaticAnalysis` and, for each single-`{param}` js-static/js-static-joined route with no observed status yet (`probeUnprobedEndpoints` itself skips any URL containing `{`, for the same "still a template" reason), substitutes a small inert id (`"1"`) and fires one bounded, anonymous GET. A 401/403 response becomes a **new** `EndpointFact` (the original `{param}` fact is untouched — idor's own candidate list is unaffected) carrying that concrete URL and status — exactly the evidence `SuggestAuthBypassPathsFromRecon`'s existing `ep.StatusCode == 401/403` branch already looks for, so that function needed no change at all. Capped at 15 probes (`maxTemplatedAuthBoundaryProbes`), same discipline as LT-76's sibling pass. Tests: `TestProbeTemplatedRouteAuthBoundary` (the real Juice Shop shape: a gated `{param}` route produces the new fact, a public one doesn't, the original template fact stays untouched), `TestProbeTemplatedRouteAuthBoundary_MultiParamPathSkipped` (a route with more than one `{...}` segment has no unambiguous substitution and is left alone).
**LT-193 (built 2026-09-22, same session) — `SuggestSQLiTargets` extended for LT-190's own query-value placeholder, regardless of key name.** The earlier diagnosis undersold this one: it isn't just that `isIDShaped("{param}")` fails — Juice Shop's real vulnerable key is `q` (a free-text search term), which also fails `looksLikeIDKey` (no "id" substring) independent of the value. SQLi's real surface was never actually ID-shaped-only the way IDOR's is; the existing two signals just happened to work for crAPI/vAPI's own query-param shapes and nobody had hit the gap. Added a third signal to `SuggestSQLiTargets`: a `js-static`/`js-static-joined` endpoint whose query value is exactly `jsTemplateLiteralPlaceholder` (`"{param}"`) is a candidate whatever the key's name — a stronger, source-declared signal than an inferred-from-observed-value one, so it doesn't need the id-name/id-shape gates at all. The literal placeholder text is never sent to the detector: `q={param}` becomes `q=` (empty value), letting `sqli.buildPayloadURL`'s own "use `1` when absent" default pick the real probe value. Tests: `TestSuggestSQLiTargets_JSTemplateLiteralQueryValue` (the real shape, placeholder text confirmed absent from the emitted path), `_WrongSourceExcluded`, `_PartialMatchExcluded` (exact match only, not substring).
**LT-192 (built and live-verified 2026-09-22, on the user's explicit "fix Body-field SQLi mode for the login-bypass gap") — a full body-field SQLi capability, mirroring LT-188(a)'s ssrf pattern end to end, plus a real error-signature gap found by live verification.** `pkg/detectors/sqli` gains `RunBodyFields`/`BodyTarget`/`WithBodyFill` (checks.go's three techniques generalized behind a new `sqliCandidate` abstraction — `probe func(ctx, suffix) (probeResult, bool)` — so the identical error/boolean/time logic serves a POST JSON body field, not just a URL query parameter; `probeBodyWithToken`/`buildPayloadBody` are the body-mode counterparts of `probeWithToken`/`buildPayloadURL`, same append-never-replace convention, `"1"+suffix` as the base). Recon gains `SuggestSQLiBodyTargets` (every `BodyParamKeys` name recon recovered is a candidate, capped at 6 per endpoint — unlike ssrf's keyword-filtered body signal, a body field's name gives no reliable "looks like SQLi" hint, so this is broad-but-bounded rather than keyword-narrowed, the same reasoning LT-193 above applies to the query-value case). The registry builds a leaf from it in `resolveEndpointFacts`, `pkg/agenttask/plantree.go` carries `SQLiBodyPath`/`SQLiBodyParams`/`SQLiBodyFillFields`/`SQLiBodyFillValues`, `planexec/executor.go` copies them into a blank config, `pkg/coverage`'s leaf-coverage tracking was extended too (`leafCoverage` now reads `SQLiBodyPath`/`SQLiBodyParams`, so LT-185's miss-attribution stays accurate for this leaf shape). `--allow-sqli-body-fill` (agent) / `--sqli-body-path`/`--sqli-body-param`/`--sqli-body-fill-field`/`--allow-sqli-body-fill` (scan, added specifically because there was otherwise no way to manually exercise this at all — recon's own auto-derivation doesn't reach Juice Shop's login fields yet, see below) is CLAUDE.md's next independently-scoped mutating exception, alongside `--allow-writes`/`--allow-mutating-bfla`/`--allow-ssrf-body-fill`: if the injection actually succeeds, reaching past a login endpoint's other-field validation can complete a real unauthorized authentication, not just a read. The three existing payload families (error/boolean-AND/time) stay unmodified and never widen access on their own — no OR-based always-true payload is in the set — so the flag's own risk is the same shape as ssrf's: reaching past validation, not the payload.

**Live-verified against the real target, and a real gap found and fixed along the way.** First live check (a standalone Go program calling `RunBodyFields` directly against Juice Shop's real `POST /rest/user/login`) found **zero findings** in both default and `WithBodyFill` mode, despite curl confirming the underlying bug is 100% real (a bare `'` in the email field produces a genuine 500 from Sequelize/SQLite) — traced by hand: Juice Shop's login route's error page is a generic Express default error page with no DBMS-specific string anywhere (`errorPatterns`' SQLite entry needs a literal `SQLITE_ERROR`/`syntax error`-shaped string, present on the already-verified `/rest/products/search` endpoint's error page but absent here) — only a Node.js/Sequelize stack-trace frame (`sequelize/lib/dialects/sqlite/query.js`) distinguishes it from an ordinary error. The boolean-based and time-based techniques were checked by hand too (curl, the exact payloads `runBodyParam` sends) and genuinely find nothing here — not a bug: this detector deliberately never includes an OR-based always-true payload, and SQLite has no `SLEEP()`, so neither technique has a signal to find on *this* backend, independent of the error-pattern gap. **Fixed:** a new `errorPatterns` entry, `Node.js/Sequelize`, matched narrowly on the dialect-runner stack frame specifically (not a bare "sequelize" mention — `SequelizeValidationError`, an app-level pre-query validation error, must never match, and a dedicated test, `TestSQLiRunBodyFields_SequelizeValidationError_NotMatched`, locks that in). **Re-verified twice more, end to end:** a standalone script confirmed the fix (`sqli-error-email-x`, high/high, even in default no-fill mode — Juice Shop's login route runs the query before checking whether a password was supplied at all, so this specific bug didn't actually need `--allow-sqli-body-fill`); the real CLI confirmed it a second time, isolated from the nuclei corpus (`hackerfive scan --detector sqli --sqli-body-path /rest/user/login --sqli-body-param email --templates <empty>`), with a full real request/response embedded in the finding's evidence. Tests: `TestSQLiRunBodyFields_ErrorBased_Hit`, `TestSQLiRunBodyFields_NodeSequelizeErrorShape_Hit` (reproduces the exact gap), `TestSQLiRunBodyFields_SequelizeValidationError_NotMatched`, `TestSQLiRunBodyFields_RequiredFieldGatesTheSignal_NeedsBodyFill` (a synthetic target that does need the fill flag, proving the flag's own effect independent of Juice Shop's specific validation order), `TestSuggestSQLiBodyTargets*` (recon side), plus the `checks.go` refactor's existing tests unchanged (8 call sites in `tests/unit/detector_sqli_test.go` and one in `timebased_test.go` updated for the new `sqliCandidate` signature, behavior unchanged — all still pass).

**Not fully closed at the time — measured, not guessed, left open on purpose.** Recon's automatic derivation of `SQLiBodyPath`/`SQLiBodyParams` (`extractJSBodyFields`, `pkg/recon/jsbody.go`, LT-186 d) did **not** yet reach Juice Shop's real login call: checked directly against the fetched bundle, `this.http.post(this.hostServer+`/rest/user/login`,e)` passes a bare variable `e` as the body, not an inline object literal the way `extractJSBodyFields`' fetch/axios pattern requires (crAPI's own shape, `fetch(url,{body:JSON.stringify({...})})`) — Angular's service-method idiom (construct the payload elsewhere, pass it in as a variable) is structurally different and would need its own extraction work (tracing `e` back to its construction site, analogous to LT-164's constant-tracing), not attempted here. So at the time the detector capability and its manual CLI surface (`--sqli-body-path`/`--sqli-body-param`) were real and live-verified; the fully-automatic `hackerfive agent` path for *this specific* login endpoint was not. **Closed 2026-09-23 — see LT-195 below.**
- **LT-195 (built and live-verified 2026-09-23, on the user's "So now with Angular bare-variable body extraction (LT-186 d follow-on), before Tier 2?") — closes LT-192's own open item: Juice Shop's login bypass is now found through the fully automatic `hackerfive agent` path, not just the manual CLI.** Turned out to be three compounding gaps, not one, each found by checking the real bundle rather than assuming the first fix was enough:
  1. **The body argument itself.** `extractJSBodyFields` had never resolved anything but an inline object literal. Juice Shop's real shape is two levels removed from that: `UserService.login(e){...this.http.post(url,e)...}` never builds a body at all — it only forwards its own parameter — and the object it forwards is built by the *caller*, one call frame up, via sequential property assignment onto an initially empty object (`this.user={},this.user.email=...,this.user.password=...,this.userService.login(this.user)`), not even an inline literal there either. Built `resolveIndirectBody` (`pkg/recon/jsbody.go`): `resolveBareObjectArg` resolves a bare variable/`this.prop` reference back to its fields, either an inline literal or that sequential-assignment shape; `enclosingFuncParam`+`resolvePassthroughCallSites` resolve a bare passthrough parameter by finding the enclosing method's own name, then searching the bundle for where *that* method is called and resolving *that* call's own argument the same way — exactly one hop, never recursive, so a chain of passthroughs longer than one is deliberately left unresolved rather than chased indefinitely (`TestExtractJSBodyFields_UnresolvablePassthrough`).
  2. **Route resolution itself.** `jsCallRoute` only ever recognized a route built from a named constant (`sg.CONTACT_MECHANIC`, crAPI's own convention) — checked against the real bundle: Juice Shop declares *zero* route constants of that shape anywhere (`grep` for the exact pattern: 0 matches in the whole 1.2 MB file). Every one of its routes is an inline literal (`` `/rest/user/login` ``), which `jsCallRoute` had no path to at all. Fixed with `jsCallRouteFromLiteral`, reusing jsstatic.go's own backtick-literal extraction/normalization so a resolved route is guaranteed to match the same literal's route key wherever jsstatic.go derives one for the identical endpoint. This also meant `extractJSBodyFields`'s own `if len(consts) == 0 { return nil }` early exit — reasonable when every bundle was assumed to declare at least one constant — was silently making the whole function dead code against a real, zero-route-constant bundle; removed.
  3. **The wiring between the two.** Even with (1) and (2) fixed, `runJSStaticAnalysis`'s `bodyByRoute` map (built from `extractJSBodyFields`) was only ever consulted inside the join mechanism's own endpoint-building loop — which itself only accepts an `"api/..."`-shaped base (`isJSAPIPathBaseCandidate` requires the first path segment to literally be `"api"`). Juice Shop's routes are `/rest/...`, so they were never eligible for the join mechanism at all and were built entirely through the separate `extractJSEndpoints`/`js-static`-source loop, which never referenced `bodyByRoute` in any form. Fixed by building `bodyByRoute` once per asset and consulting it in *both* loops (`pkg/recon/jsstatic.go`), keyed by `strings.TrimPrefix(epURL, assetHost)` for the direct-endpoint loop.

  None of the three is Juice-Shop-specific: (1) is Angular's own standard service/component split, (2) is any app that inlines its routes rather than naming them as constants (arguably more common than crAPI's own convention), (3) is any bundle whose routes don't happen to start with `"api/"`.

  Live-verified against the real bundle throughout, each fix checked before assuming the next was sufficient (the "not just guess the next thing" discipline this whole session used caught (2) and (3), which the original LT-192 write-up didn't know existed): a standalone extraction check against the real fetched `main.js` first returned 0 results even after (1) alone; tracing why found (2); fixing (2) surfaced (3) once route resolution succeeded but still produced no `EndpointFact.BodyParamKeys`. With all three: `hackerfive plan -t http://localhost:3000 --recon-depth full` (no manual flags at all) builds a real `sqli` leaf, `sqli_body_path: "/rest/user/login"`, `sqli_body_params: ["email","password","oauth"]` — recon-derived, not hand-supplied. The real, fully-automatic `hackerfive agent -t http://localhost:3000 --recon-depth full --auth-token <A> --other-auth-token <B> --no-model --run-every-leaf` (two fresh throwaway accounts, isolated from the nuclei corpus) dispatched that leaf on its own and found `sqli-error-email-x`, `high`/`high`, a real Node.js/Sequelize error, through the actual orchestrated path — not a hand-built `--sqli-body-path`/`--sqli-body-param`.

  Tests: `TestExtractJSBodyFields_JuiceShopLoginPassthrough` (the exact real shape, trimmed), `TestExtractJSBodyFields_InlineLiteralAssignedToBareVariable`, `TestExtractJSBodyFields_UnresolvablePassthrough` (proves the one-hop bound), `TestExtractJSBodyFields_ReservedWordNotMistakenForMethodName` (`if(e){` vs. a real one-param method definition), `TestRunWave3_JSStatic_BodyFieldsReachAbsolutePathEndpoint` (the wiring fix, end to end through `runJSStaticAnalysis`); all pre-existing `jsbody`/`jsstatic` tests re-verified passing, with two updated to reflect genuinely new, correct behavior rather than the old absence of it (`TestExtractJSBodyFields_Variants`'s "a url that resolves to no route constant" case now resolves via the literal itself, exactly as designed).

  `go build`/`go vet`/`go vet -tags eval`/`golangci-lint run ./...`: clean. `go test ./... -race`: clean except the pre-existing, unrelated `pkg/scriptexec` LT-181 failure. `tests/fixtures/known-vulns/juiceshop.json`'s `juiceshop-sqli-login-bypass` note updated to record the fully-automatic path; `docs/20-setup-testing-targets.md`'s Juice Shop section updated to drop the "not yet fully automatic" caveat.
- **LT-196 (measured 2026-09-23, on the user's "what's next, Tier 2 or else?" — re-ran the full ablation harness fresh across all three labs now that LT-194/LT-195 are in, since J1/J2's own deferral was conditioned on "no measured miss beyond deterministic work" and that condition needed re-checking against current code, not assumed to still hold.** All four arms (`no-model`, `no-model+all-leaves`, `model-every-turn`, `fast-lane+model`), one run each, against crAPI, vAPI and Juice Shop, each with two throwaway accounts freshly provisioned and containers rebuilt clean. **Results:** crAPI's `crapi-ssrf-contact-mechanic` is `7-found` in every dispatching arm (LT-194 holds through the real orchestrated path); Juice Shop's known-vuln recall goes from 0% reachable through any agent arm (pre-fix) to **100% in every non-trivial arm** (LT-195 holds completely — `juiceshop-sqli-login-bypass` found in `no-model+all-leaves`, `model-every-turn` and `fast-lane+model` alike); the model arm never beats the deterministic one on any of the three labs (crAPI 71% vs 71%, vAPI 33% vs 33% with the *same* vuln found, Juice Shop 100% vs 100%). What's left (`crapi-bola-vehicle-location`, `crapi-bfla-delete-video`, `vapi-jwt-alg-none`, `vapi-ssrf-serversurfer`) is the same set doc94's "second lab" section already diagnosed — either an explicit human-approval gate or genuinely unlinked/unserved evidence a model has no more to reason from than the deterministic path already does. **So J1/J2 stays not-yet-justified, measured fresh rather than assumed** — full table and reading in doc94's new "Full-suite re-measurement after LT-194/LT-195" section. **Infra note, not a HackerFive bug:** the harness's own 60m global `-timeout` wasn't enough headroom to fit Juice Shop's four arms (its `no-model+all-leaves` alone took 2334s) in one `go test` invocation; the two model arms were re-run separately with `-timeout 100m` and `HACKERFIVE_ABLATION_ARMS` — each run's own result is written to the JSONL as it finishes, so the first, timed-out invocation's two completed arms stayed valid. **Suggested next deterministic candidate, not yet investigated:** `crapi-bola-vehicle-location` may be a recon gap rather than a served-evidence gap like vAPI's two (worth checking whether crAPI actually links a `/location` route anywhere) before assuming it's in the same "nothing to reason from" bucket.
- **LT-197 (fixed 2026-09-23, same day it was found live-reverifying the LT-166 fix against `*.nettix.com.pe`) — a specific-template leaf's "fast" id:-peek load isn't fast: it walks and reads the entire template corpus, every single dispatch, with no early exit.** After LT-166's fix, a second `TestAblationRealScope` run against `*.nettix.com.pe` was still dominated by per-leaf cost: `chasqui04.nettix.com.pe`'s leaves 3-6 each took 68-89s despite `loaded 1 nuclei-compatible, 0 native templates`. `hackerfive plan` against that one host directly (a real, live call) showed why: 13 of its ~15 leaves are distinct specific-template leaves from tech fingerprinting (`nginx-api-traversal`, `nginx-merge-slashes-path-traversal`, `git-config-nginxoffbyslash`, `CVE-2021-20086`, ... — real, correct leaves, one per matched template), and `RunPlan`'s own design correctly gives every specific-template leaf its own corpus load (`pkg/planexec/executor.go`'s "a specific-template leaf always keeps the corpus — it needs a full load to resolve its one id:" — LT-166's fix deliberately doesn't touch this branch, it's not the bug). The bug is in what that load actually costs: `pkg/scanner/engine.go` routes a `TemplateID`-only leaf through `fastIDs != nil` → `nuclei.LoadDirByIDs(dir, fastIDs)` (`pkg/template/nuclei/loader.go:97`), documented in-code as the "F4 (LT-71) id:-peek fast path" — but `LoadDirByIDs` is a `filepath.WalkDir` over the *entire* directory, `os.ReadFile`-ing every `.yaml`/`.yml` file (peeking its `id:` only after the full read, and only skipping the YAML-unmarshal step for a non-match), with **no early exit once every wanted ID is found** and **no LT-106 parse-cache participation at all** (`tagCacheEligible := fastIDs == nil && ...` explicitly excludes this path, and `storeParseCache` is only called `if fastIDs == nil`). Directly timed: a bare `filepath.WalkDir` + `os.ReadFile` pass over `.nuclei-templates-cache` (~9,650 files) took ~32s by itself, live-measured on this box — `.nuclei-templates-cache` lives under the repo's `/mnt/c` Windows mount, so WSL2's cross-filesystem per-file I/O overhead makes this worse here than it would be on a native filesystem, but the algorithmic issue (whole-corpus walk-and-read per leaf, unconditionally, every dispatch, regardless of `want`'s size or how many of the wanted IDs have already been found) is real on any filesystem — it's O(corpus size) work to resolve O(1) template IDs. **Practical impact (before the fix below):** this, not LT-166, was what blocked LT-183(j)'s real-scope measurement from completing in practice — any host with several tech-fingerprint-matched templates (common: this is nettix's normal case, not a corner case) paid this cost once per matched template, not once per host.

  **Fixed 2026-09-23, approach (b).** `nuclei.LoadDirByIDsWithIndex` (`pkg/template/nuclei/loader.go`) is the same single walk `LoadDirByIDs` already did, plus a second return: `allIDs`, the id→dir-relative-path of *every* template file the walk observed, not only the wanted one(s) — free, since the walk already peeks every file's `id:` to decide whether it's wanted. `pkg/scanner.Engine.storeIDIndex` merges `allIDs` into the LT-106 sidecar (id-only entries — `Tags`/`Severity` blank, `mergeIDOnlyEntries` never downgrades an existing tag-usable entry), and `Engine.loadDirViaIDIndex` is `loadDirViaParseCache`'s id:-based counterpart, selecting by exact ID membership (new `selectCachedByIDs`, all-or-nothing like its tag-based sibling) instead of tag intersection — a hit resolves via `nuclei.LoadFiles` (parses only the wanted files) with **no directory walk at all**. `loadTemplates` now tries this cache before falling back to `LoadDirByIDsWithIndex`'s walk-and-collect, which then warms the index for the next specific-template leaf against the same dir (this run, or a later one — the sidecar is disk-persisted).

  **A second, larger cost was found and fixed alongside it:** `native.LoadDirDetailed(dir)` — a *second*, completely separate, uncached full-corpus walk-and-parse-attempt — ran unconditionally after the nuclei load, on **every** leaf dispatch, id-cache path or not, and measured as the *larger* of the two costs on a real corpus (a live re-run with only the nuclei-side fix still showed leaves 3-5 at 70-90s each, entirely attributable to this). It's skipped entirely on the id-cache-eligible path: a `TemplateID`-narrowed leaf's `fastIDs` are always drawn from `registry`'s synced-corpus template-tag selection, never a native (first-party, `templates/idor/`) template — a native template dispatches through the tag-scoped corpus pass instead, which sets `Tags`/`DerivedTags` and so never takes this branch at all (`fastLoadNucleiIDs` returns nil whenever either is set). If that assumption is ever wrong for a given id, the existing `nucleiIDsCovered` fallback still catches it (falls back to a genuine full parse, nuclei + native) rather than silently missing a match — proven with a dedicated test (`TestLoadTemplates_IDCacheEligible_SkipsNativeLoad_FallsBackIfActuallyNeeded`), not just asserted.

  **Live-reverified against the exact host that exposed this (`chasqui04.nettix.com.pe`, `*.nettix.com.pe`, parse cache cleared each time):** leaf 1 (cold — no index yet, unavoidable) still pays the walk once, ~225-270s depending on the run; every leaf after it dropped from **68-90s to 20-48s each** — a genuine 2-4x improvement, each confirmed via `id index hit ... without walking the corpus (LT-197)` in the live log, not inferred. New unit tests: `TestSelectCachedByIDs`, `TestMergeIDOnlyEntries`, `TestParseCache_IDIndex_MissThenHitForADifferentID` (`pkg/scanner/parsecache_test.go`), `TestLoadDirByIDsWithIndex_ReturnsFullIndexAsAByproduct`, `TestLoadDirByIDsWithIndex_EmptyWant` (`tests/unit/nuclei_loader_idlist_test.go`). `go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./... -race` clean (the pre-existing, unrelated `pkg/scriptexec` LT-181 environment failure aside).

  **Honest residual, not yet fixed — logged rather than chased further this round:** the remaining 20-48s/leaf is now dominated by a *different*, pre-existing, shared mechanism: `fingerprintTemplateDir` (LT-106) — a stat-only `filepath.WalkDir` over the whole corpus, run fresh on every `loadDirViaIDIndex`/`loadDirViaParseCache` call to validate the cache is still current. This is an existing design LT-197 reused as-is, not something this fix introduced, but on this environment (`.nuclei-templates-cache` under the repo's `/mnt/c` Windows mount) even a stat-only pass over ~9,650 files carries real per-call WSL2 cross-filesystem latency. A plausible next step: cache the fingerprint itself in-process for the life of one `Engine`/agent run (it cannot change mid-run — the corpus directory isn't being edited while a scan is in flight), so only the *first* load of a given dir pays the stat-walk instead of every one. Not built here — a genuinely separate item from both the id-cache and native-skip work above, and this round's fix already delivers the bulk of the win (eliminating the redundant *content* walk, which was the dominant cost) without it.
- **LT-184 (fixed 2026-09-21; root cause below) — recon builds 12 `idor` leaves from one `report_id` query key, spread over unrelated paths.** The control's undispatched leaves were `idor …?report_id={{id}}` on `/identity/api/auth/{login,signup,forget-password,unlock,verify,v3/check-otp,v4.0/user/login-with-token}` and `/workshop/api/mechanic{,/download_report,/receive_report,/service_requests}`, plus the real `/workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report`. Only the last is an object endpoint. The key looks like it was attached to many paths from one real observation (suspects: LT-95's empty-ID-key templating, or LT-164's service-prefix + API-path join; not confirmed). **Cost:** a decision turn each for a model, or 12 scans for any run-everything policy, and a misleading candidate list for anything that reasons over it. **Fix:** find where the key is attached; require per-path evidence (the key observed on that path, or documented for it in a spec) before an `idor` leaf is built. **Root cause and fix (2026-09-21):** `pkg/recon/jsstatic.go`'s LT-164 join step verified prefix+base route pairs and then crossed every keyless `?key=` literal in the bundle with every verified route, so one real `?report_id=` became a look-alike on each of the other verified routes (7 under `/identity/api/auth/` — POST-only routes answer 405, which passes the join check — and 4 under `/workshop/api/mechanic`). The bundle declares a route under a property name (`GET_SERVICE_REPORT:"api/mechanic/mechanic_report"`) and completes it where used (`sg.GET_SERVICE_REPORT+"?report_id="`), so `assignQuerySuffixes` now ties a suffix to the route whose declaring property name is immediately followed by the literal (names under 3 characters are ignored, since a minifier's mangled locals recur everywhere); a suffix tied to nothing is attached only when exactly one route verified, else dropped; untied routes are emitted bare. **Verified live on crAPI:** one `idor` leaf instead of 12, `download_report` keeps its own `?filename=`, and the run-every-leaf arm dropped from 194 s to 44 s with identical findings (15, 2 of 2 expected, 2 of 7 known); the model arm went from 15 to 6 model turns and $0.017 to $0.0057. Regression tests: `TestAssignQuerySuffixes_*`, `TestRunWave3_JSStaticAnalysis_QuerySuffixOnlyOnItsOwnRoute`. **Limit:** the tie relies on the bundle using a named property; an inline literal or a template string is not tied and is dropped when several routes verified. That trades some recall for precision, and no lab here has that shape.
- **LT-185 (built 2026-09-21, first result recorded; the measurement half of J1/J2/J5) — a missed known vulnerability is reported as "missed", not as *where the pipeline lost it*.** The baseline says 5 of 7 known vulnerabilities were missed by every arm, but the reasons (no leaf built, LT-180's empty `protected_paths`, unreachable by design) were worked out by hand from a tree dump, and one is still "unverified". Without the stage, J1/J2 cannot be aimed at a measured gap and their success cannot be scored ("moved 3 vulnerabilities from stage 1 to a finding"). **Stages, in pipeline order:** (0) recon never observed the endpoint; (1) observed, but no leaf was built for it (registry/decision engine produced nothing, or attached the wrong parameter, cf. LT-184); (2) leaf built but blocked on a required field (idor `EndpointTemplate`, authbypass `ProtectedPaths`, ssrf params — LT-138/LT-180); (3) runnable but never dispatched; (4) dispatched, detector raised nothing (detector limit or wrong parameter); (5) a finding was raised but does not match the known entry (ground-truth mismatch); (6) unreachable by design (gated mutating class). **Build:** classify each `known-vulns` entry from what one run already produces — `Result.Tree` leaf targets/detectors/fields/status and the history's skip reasons — plus one new input the stream does not carry today: recon's endpoint list (add a `recon` stream event or `--dump-recon <file>`: URL, method, source, auth flag — no values). Report the furthest stage reached per known vulnerability and arm, and a "lost at stage N" histogram per arm. **Keep the classifier a library, not harness-only code:** the same function over recon + tree + history with no ground truth is J5's coverage ledger (endpoint x class rows, each carrying a stage), so the harness proves it on labs and the agent uses it on real targets. **Expected first result** (hypotheses to check, not conclusions): shop-orders BOLA, vehicle-location BOLA and the `contact_mechanic` SSRF land at stage 0 or 1 (which of the two is exactly what J1's design depends on); authbypass-jwt at 2 or 4 (settles LT-180's "unverified"); DELETE-video at 6. **Second mode:** also report a stage for *every* recon endpoint against the class list, so the size of the miss population can be estimated on labs and targets that have no ground truth. **Built 2026-09-21:** `pkg/coverage` (library: `Attribute` for a known target, `EndpointStages` for every recon endpoint, stages 0-7 with `7 found`); `orchestrator.Result.Recon` carries recon's endpoints (method, URL with data-shaped path segments collapsed to `{id}` and query values dropped, source, status, auth flag, parameter names; capped at 5000, `ReconDropped` counts the rest); the harness stores `known_stages`, `known_why`, `endpoint_stages` and the sorted finding IDs per run and prints a per-arm table; `crapi.json` gained a `gated` field for the DELETE-video entry. **First result (crAPI, 1 run per arm, doc94 "Miss attribution"):** shop-orders and vehicle-location BOLA at stage 0, `contact_mechanic` SSRF at stage 1, `authbypass-jwt` at stage 2 (LT-180 confirmed: skipped for want of `--protected-paths`), DELETE-video gated; none is a selection loss. **Not done:** the class-by-endpoint ledger for J5 (needs an applicability rule per class, which is J1's job); stage 5 is a hint, not a proof (a same-class finding on another endpoint may mean the fixture is wrong); only crAPI has ground truth, so only crAPI has stages per vulnerability, while endpoint mode works on any lab.
- **LT-186 (all four items done 2026-09-21; cause verified 2026-09-21) — recon's JS route extraction drops 26 of the 41 API routes in crAPI's bundle, including two known-vulnerable ones, and derives no request-body field from JS.** Two of crAPI's documented vulnerabilities are lost at stage 0 (`/workshop/api/shop/orders/{id}`, the vehicle-location route, whose path contains `/location`: no recon endpoint contains either) and one at stage 1 (`/workshop/api/merchant/contact_mechanic` is observed via the JS join, but its body-field names come only from an API spec, `EndpointFact.BodyParamKeys`, and crAPI serves none). **Cause, verified against the served bundle (41 `api/` route literals):** (1) `extractJSPathJoinParts` sorts the base candidates alphabetically and keeps 15 (`maxJSPathBases`), so 18 are never considered — everything from `api/shop/orders/all` on, all of `api/v2/*` included; (2) 8 are rejected because a `<orderId>`-style placeholder fails `IsPlausibleURLPath` (`jsSyntaxInPath` treats angle brackets as JS syntax): `api/shop/orders/<orderId>`, `api/v2/vehicle/<carId>/location`, `api/v2/community/posts/<postId>{,/comment}`, `api/v2/user/videos/<videoId>`, `api/mechanic/service_request/<serviceId>{,/comment}`, `api/merchant/service_requests/<vehicleVIN>`; (3) a placeholder route cannot be verified by the join's live GET as written (`workshop/api/shop/orders/<orderId>` answers 404 with or without a token, the same as the canary; `identity/api/v2/vehicle/<carId>/location` answers 401 anonymous and 400 authenticated). No authenticated crawl is needed for either route. **Downstream check:** given the leaf `/workshop/api/shop/orders/{{id}}`, `idor` finds the 5 known high findings. The vehicle route also needs a UUID seed, and the only place a real one appears is the authenticated `GET /identity/api/v2/vehicle/vehicles` response (the owner account has one vehicle, the other none); recon keeps shapes, not values. **Work, deterministic, in order of cost:** (a) accept `<name>` placeholders in route literals and convert them to `{name}` (`isSpecPathParam` already yields an `{{id}}` candidate from that form), and verify a templated route by a method that invents no value, e.g. its static ancestor route or the prefix canary diff, not by requesting the literal placeholder; (b) replace the alphabetical 15-base cap with a probe budget spent per prefix (41 bases x 4 prefixes is 164 pairs against `maxJSJoinProbes` 120), and warn when candidates are cut rather than dropping them silently; (c) id harvesting for UUID-keyed routes: read id-shaped values from an authenticated list GET into memory as seeds, never into the stream or results file, which needs the privacy review doc94 section 7 already flags; (d) request-body field names from the bundle for the stage-1 SSRF case, populating `BodyParamKeys` when there is no spec; (e) a model-proposed route probe (J1) only for routes present in no client artifact, validated by a status-code check and never counted as a finding. An authenticated crawl is not needed on this evidence and is deprioritised. **Success measure:** LT-185's stage table: vulnerabilities moved from stage 0/1 to a finding. **Done 2026-09-21 (a and b):** `normalizeJSPathPlaceholders` turns a whole-segment `<name>` into `{name}` before the plausibility check; `collectJSPathJoinParts` returns every candidate and `capStrings` reports what a cap cuts; `maxJSPathBases` 15 to 80; `verifyJSJoinBases` replaces the pair cross product: a plain base is probed under prefixes (the prefix that verified its nearest sibling first) and stops at the first that verifies, one prefix per route, budget `maxJSJoinProbes` 300 counted in requests; a templated base is never requested and borrows the prefix of a verified plain base sharing all its leading static segments (at least two), else is counted and warned. Found while testing: a route that answers 500 to a parameterless authenticated request (crAPI `mechanic_report`) was dropped whenever recon carried a token, so 500 now verifies (502/503/504 do not). Live on crAPI: joined routes 14 to 23 without a token and to 38 with one; shop-orders BOLA found (doc94 "After the LT-186 fixes"); known vulnerabilities 2 of 7 to 3 of 7.
