Tests: `TestRunWave3_JSJoin_*`, `TestCollectJSPathJoinParts_*`, `TestPreferSiblingPrefix`, `TestNormalizeJSPathPlaceholders`. **Limits:** the two-segment sibling rule leaves a parameterised route unverified when its resource family has no verified plain route (`api/mechanic/service_request/{id}` on crAPI, whose sibling is `service_requests`), reported in a recon warning; one prefix per route assumes a route lives in one service. **Done 2026-09-21 (c and d, plus what they needed):** (c) `harvestUUIDSeed`/`attachHarvestedSeeds` (`pkg/recon/seeds.go`): the shape probe lifts one own-id UUID per list response and matches it to a templated route by resource stem and shared service prefix; it lives in `EndpointFact.SeedID` and `PlanNode.HarvestedSeedID`, both `json:"-"`, is read only by planexec's idor dispatch, and the ledger records `Templated`/`Seeded` flags only (tests assert the id is absent from the tree JSON, the recon result JSON and the ledger JSON). (d) `extractJSBodyFields` (`pkg/recon/jsbody.go`): route, body field names and the URL-carrying field from `fetch`/`.post` calls whose URL resolves to a route constant, onto `EndpointFact.BodyParamKeys`/`URLBodyParamKeys`, which `SuggestSSRFBodyParamsFromRecon` includes whatever the name. Also: verified joined routes now keep their status and `AuthRequired` (an anonymous 401/403, with one credential-free GET per route when recon is signed in), which feeds authbypass protected paths (see LT-180). Found while testing: the ssrf leaf named parameters but not the endpoint, so its probes went to the host root (`SSRFPath`, one leaf per endpoint, `SuggestSSRFTargets`); and the tree's dedup key collapsed every endpoint-driven ssrf leaf, and every sqli leaf, into one (two sqli candidate paths gave one leaf), fixed for both. Live on crAPI (doc94 "Closing the deterministic gaps"): known vulnerabilities 3 of 7 to 5 of 7 with `--recon-auth`, 4 of 7 without. **Limits:** the seed is one id per list and is assumed to be the caller's own (a list that returns other users' objects would seed a resource the caller cannot read, which only makes idor find nothing); the JS body reader is a pattern match over minified code (fetch/axios with an inline object literal) and yields nothing otherwise; a UUID-keyed route whose list is not reachable by a parameterless GET stays unseeded, and the ledger now says so (stage 4, "no seed").
- **LT-187 (opt-in `--recon-auth` built, measured, and extended to the web UI and MCP 2026-09-21; the default stays off; found while verifying LT-186) — `hackerfive agent --auth-token` reaches the leaf dispatches but not recon; recon carries only `--header`.** The web UI's agent launch and the MCP `plan`/`recon` tools build recon the same way (operator headers only). Effect measured on crAPI with the same code: an unauthenticated recon verified 23 joined routes, an authenticated one 38; identity's whole `api/v2/*` family (`vehicle/vehicles`, `user/*`, `user/videos`) and `vehicle/{carId}/location` only appear with a token, because that service answers 401 to everything anonymous, so the canary diff cannot separate real routes from unknown ones. The same gap applies to the response-shape probe (Wave 3 GETs use operator headers only), so J1's input is also unauthenticated. `HACKERFIVE_ABLATION_RECON_AUTH=1` (harness only) now sends the owner token to recon so the effect can be measured. **Options:** (1) an opt-in `--recon-auth` on `agent` that applies the `--auth-token`/`--auth-header-*` header to recon; (2) default it on whenever `--auth-token` is given, with a stderr note. **Risk to weigh, and why this is not simply switched on:** recon is read-only GET, but a crawl carrying a live session can hit a state-changing GET (a logout link that invalidates a cookie session), and it widens what the operator's token touches; the scope gates are unchanged. Recommend (1) first, measure on crAPI and one non-token lab, then decide the default. Per the project's unattended-run preference a default-on with a logged warning is acceptable, but this one changes what a credential is used for, so it needs an explicit decision. **Done 2026-09-21:** `hackerfive agent --recon-auth` (needs `--auth-token`; header name/format from `--auth-header-name`/`--auth-header-format`, default `Authorization: Bearer`) sends the credential on recon's own requests to the target's host only. `httpclient.WithHostHeaders(origin, headers)` keys on host+port, works on a request clone (so a redirect never carries it, unlike `net/http`'s own copy of a custom header), tested against a second origin, a redirect and default-port/case/subdomain/lookalike hosts, with the clone removal mutation-checked; `recon.WithCrawlHeaders(origin, headers)` puts it on the katana crawl only when every seed is on that origin and pins the crawl with `-fs fqdn`, never on httpx; a crawl left unauthenticated for that reason says so in a recon warning. A stderr note names the header and origin (never the token). **Measured on crAPI (doc94 "Authenticated recon (LT-187)"):** recon endpoints 55 to 69 and vehicle-location observed, but the same 20 findings and 3 of 7 known vulnerabilities as without it. So the benefit shown so far is coverage, not findings; it is the precondition for LT-186 item (c) (the UUID seed), not a fix for it. **Still open:** the default (stay opt-in until a lab shows it changing findings without the new guard being enough; on crAPI it now changes findings: 4 of 7 to 5 of 7, via the seed and the identity routes); the web UI agent launch and the MCP `plan`/`recon` tools do not have the option yet; other labs (a cookie-session app would be the informative case; a cookie session already works through `--auth-header-name Cookie --auth-header-format 'session={token}'`, but has not been run against a real one). **Correction 2026-09-21:** an earlier version of this entry said the response-shape probe was authenticated by the flag automatically because it "goes through the same client". That was wrong: `probeResponseShapes` builds its own no-redirect client, so it ran anonymously. It now wraps its transport with the credential (`Recon.credentialed`); the LT-76 status probe stays anonymous on purpose (its 401 is the protected-path signal). **Also done 2026-09-21:** state-changing-GET guard (`recon.StateChangingURL`: logout/signout/delete/remove/revoke/destroy/deactivate/unsubscribe/terminate/cancel/purge/wipe/reset as whole path or query tokens): such a URL is requested without the credential and the katana crawl excludes it (`-cos`); one shared `recon.Credential` (NewCredential/Middleware/Option/Note) so the CLI, web UI and MCP cannot drift; web UI: an unchecked, never-remembered "Also use the auth token for recon" box, refused at submit without a token; MCP: an operator-only environment switch `HACKERFIVE_RECON_AUTH=1` on the server (deliberately not a tool parameter: `plan` runs recon before the human approval prompt, so a model-settable flag would authenticate recon with no gate), token from `auth_token` or `HACKERFIVE_AUTH_TOKEN`, a warning in the result says whether recon was authenticated.
- **LT-188 (open, found 2026-09-21 while closing LT-186) — agent-mode SSRF on crAPI's `contact_mechanic` is dispatched at the right endpoint and still raises nothing.** Two causes, neither in recon. **(a) The detector's request body carries only the SSRF field.** `checkBodyParamTargets` posts `{"mechanic_api": <payload>}` alone (its doc comment names this as an accepted limitation "until live testing shows it matters"; it does). Live against crAPI: that body gets `400 {"message":"Could not connect to mechanic api."}` for `http://127.0.0.1/`, which no detector marker recognises, while a full body (the field names LT-186 (d) now recovers, `mechanic_code`, `problem_details`, `vin`, ...) with `mechanic_api` pointed at a live URL returns `{"response_from_mechanic_api": "<the fetched body>", "status": 500}`: an in-band SSRF whose tell is a response field named after the fetch. Options: send the other recovered body keys with neutral values; add a marker for a response field that echoes the fetched content (a J2-shaped hypothesis if it stays too route-specific for a rule). **(b) `hackerfive agent` never runs the blind out-of-band check.** `scan` defaults `--oob-server` to the public Interactsh pool (opt out with `--no-oob`), but the agent configures no OOB server, so an SSRF that only a callback can prove is never proven in agent mode. Mirroring `scan`'s default sends only a payload URL to a public server (the tradeoff `scan` already accepted, follow-up §1), so it is the same decision, but it is a third-party data flow and needs an explicit yes. **(b) done 2026-09-21 on the user's explicit yes:** `hackerfive agent` now has `--oob-server` (default `ssrf.DefaultOOBServers`, the same two public servers as `scan`) and `--no-oob`, threaded through `BaseScanConfig.OOBServers`; a stderr line at start names the servers and says the target's address and callback time are visible to that operator, or says `--no-oob` skipped the check, because an unattended run has nobody to notice otherwise. Test: `TestAgentCmd_OOBDefaultMirrorsScan`. **Not done:** MCP `scan`/`plan` never set `OOBServers` at all (already true before this, so MCP has never run the blind check); the web UI agent launch shares the scan form's default and was not re-verified. If MCP gets it, the `plan` approval text must name the third-party server, because a model-invoked tool is where nobody is watching. **(a) measured 2026-09-21 by hand against crAPI:** the single-field body gets the same `400 "Could not connect to mechanic api."` for an unfetchable URL *and* for a reachable one, so crAPI validates the other required fields before it tries any fetch, and no oracle over that body can see a fetch. With a full body (`mechanic_code`, `problem_details`, `vin` from the caller's own vehicle list, `repeat_request_if_failed`, `number_of_repeats`) the response carries `response_from_mechanic_api` echoing what was fetched (the lab's web page, an internal error page, or JSON), in-band, and an unreachable URL still gets the generic 400. **Side effect:** pointing it at crAPI's own `receive_report` created a persisted report (id 10), so filling the other fields completes the endpoint's real action; a full-body probe is a state-changing capability, so it needs its own scoped flag (CLAUDE.md). **(a) done 2026-09-21, off by default on the user's explicit choice:** `--allow-ssrf-body-fill` (agent-only; `scan` never derives `SSRFBodyFillFields` from recon at all, so it has nothing to fill), gated the same way `--allow-writes`/`--allow-mutating-bfla` are — skipped with a warning when fields were recovered but the flag is absent, never silently run. `ssrf.WithBodyFill(fields, values)` fills every recovered field but the one under test; `values` lets a field whose bundle value is a simple `true`/`false`/integer literal (`pkg/recon/jsbody.go`'s `jsValueLiteral`, including the `!0`/`!1` minifier idiom) be sent verbatim instead of the generic placeholder string, because crAPI's other two fields are strictly typed and reject a string on either (`{"repeat_request_if_failed":["Must be a valid boolean."],"number_of_repeats":["A valid integer is required."]}` — found live, the placeholder-only version silently produced nothing). Recovery threads through `EndpointFact.BodyParamLiterals` → `recon.SSRFTarget.FillValues` → `PlanNode.SSRFBodyFillValues` → `scanner.Config.SSRFBodyFillValues`, the same shape `SSRFBodyFillFields` already used. The payload set in this mode is also capped to one per family (an internal address, a cloud-metadata path, a scheme payload) instead of the ~15-payload full sweep, since every probe may be a live write; the internal address is the target's own origin (`bodyFillSelfOriginHost`), not a fixed `127.0.0.1`, since a multi-container deployment's own loopback is often not where its outbound calls can reach (found live: crAPI's workshop container has nothing on `127.0.0.1`). Tests: `TestSSRFBodyParamTarget_WithBodyFill_*`, `TestJSValueLiteral`, `TestRunWave3_JSJoin_BodyFieldsReachTheSSRFSuggestion`, `TestApplyLeafReconFields_SSRFBodyFillFieldsCopied`. **Verified by hand (not by the CLI — see limit below):** with the exact body the detector now builds (placeholder `vin`/`mechanic_code`/`problem_details`, literal `false`/`1`), a payload pointed at a reachable docker-internal host (`crapi-web`) returns the real SSRF echo (`response_from_mechanic_api` carrying the fetched homepage); pointed at an unreachable one, the same generic 400 as before — the fill mechanism and the literal-value fix both work exactly as designed. **Limit, found live and left open:** `hackerfive agent --allow-ssrf-body-fill` against the running crAPI lab still raises nothing, because the one capped internal payload (the target's own origin, `localhost:8888`) is a host-mapped port — from inside crAPI's workshop container, `localhost` resolves to itself, which listens on nothing at 8888, so that probe gets "could not connect" same as an unreachable one. The docker-internal hostname that *would* work (`crapi-web`) is not discoverable from anything recon can legitimately see, and hardcoding it would violate CLAUDE.md's rule against biasing generic code to one lab's specific name. This is a property of this lab's docker-compose networking, not of the fix; a real target whose public hostname is also its own reachable origin (the common case outside a NAT'd multi-container lab) would not hit it. Also noted: the dispatch-shape defects found on the way are fixed (an ssrf leaf without an endpoint path; the tree's dedup collapsing every endpoint-driven ssrf and sqli leaf).
- **LT-194 (built and live-verified 2026-09-23) — closes LT-188 (a)'s remaining half: the detector's own response check required exactly HTTP 200, so crAPI's real reflected fetch was invisible regardless of what its body held.** LT-188 (a) fixed getting a real fetch attempted (past `contact_mechanic`'s field validation); this is the separate, narrower gap that was still open after it — "add a marker for a response field that echoes the fetched content," listed as an option in LT-188's own first paragraph, never built until now. **Root cause:** `looksFetched` (checks.go) gated every check on `resp.StatusCode == http.StatusOK`, and `fetchBaseline`/`fetchBodyBaseline` discarded the baseline entirely (`ok: false`) whenever the *unreachable*-control probe didn't answer 200 either — so a target whose own convention for "fetch failed/rejected" is itself non-200 (crAPI answers `400 "Could not connect to mechanic api."`) got no usable baseline and no eligible response, no matter what a real payload's response looked like. **Fix (`pkg/detectors/ssrf/checks.go`):** `probeBaseline` now captures any status the probe completes with (not just 200); `divergesFromBaseline` replaces the old `baselineMatches` — a payload's response counts as evidence when it diverges from its own baseline by status code *or* body length (`baselineTolerancePct`, unchanged), not only when it happens to be a plain 200. No baseline (the control itself failed at the transport level) falls back to the old absolute check (200 + non-trivial body), conservative by design. Also added: the target's own origin (`bodyFillSelfOriginHost`, already LT-188 a's one WithBodyFill internal-target payload) is now in the *default* (non-fill, GET and POST) internal-target sweep too — none of the RFC1918/loopback samples already there are reliably reachable from inside a real container, and a plain read-only GET probe carries none of WithBodyFill's live-write risk, so it belongs outside the flag. Also fixed a latent bug found while doing this: `checkBodyParamTargets`' non-fill branch (`len(fill) == 0`) unconditionally *replaced* `internalPayloads` with the RFC1918/loopback list, silently dropping the self-origin entry that the same line's un-taken branch already had — self-origin is now appended, not replaced. **False positive found and fixed by live verification, not guessed:** the first live run against crAPI's real `contact_mechanic` (no `--allow-ssrf-body-fill`, single-field body — `mechanic_api` is the endpoint's only actually-mandatory field, confirmed from `ContactMechanicSerializer`) returned 4 findings, all "high" confidence: 1 genuine (the self-origin probe reached a live server and got a real, distinct 404 "Not Found" response — confirmed by hand with curl, byte-for-byte reproducible), and 3 false (`file://`/`gopher://`/`dict://` scheme payloads each crashed the endpoint into Django's generic "Server Error (500)" template via an uncaught `InvalidSchema` exception — the view's `except (MissingSchema, InvalidURL)` clause doesn't catch it — which also diverges from the baseline and also contains a generic `<html` marker, but proves nothing was actually fetched; confirmed by hand that all three crash bodies are byte-identical, never file/Redis/Memcached content). The existing marker list (`looksLikeFetchedContent`) couldn't tell these apart: `<html`/`<!DOCTYPE` are true of both a real fetched page and a generic crash page. **Fixed:** split the marker list into `distinctiveFetchMarkers` (root:, redis_version, STAT , instance-id, computeMetadata — specific enough to trust at any status) and `genericHTMLMarkers` (`<html`, `<!DOCTYPE` — only trusted at a 2xx status now); `confidenceFor` takes the response's status code. Re-verified after the fix: same 4 findings, now correctly "low" confidence across the board (none of the four productions is a 2xx or carries a distinctive marker) — genuinely uncertain signal, honestly triaged, not suppressed and not overclaimed. **Also found, a correction to LT-188's own record:** that entry's "Limit, found live and left open" paragraph says `localhost:8888` (recon's view of the target's own origin) gets "could not connect" from inside crAPI's workshop container. Re-tested today with the same container, same account, same field: it does *not* — `{"mechanic_api":"http://localhost:8888/"}` returns a real HTTP 404 with a distinct body, different from the unreachable baseline's 400. Left unexplained (a docker-networking difference between sessions, not investigated further) rather than silently overwritten; both observations are on record now. Tests: `TestSSRFInternalTarget_NonOKStatusReflection_Hit`, `TestSSRFInternalTarget_DistinctiveMarker_HighConfidenceEvenOnErrorStatus`, `TestSSRFInternalTarget_GenericMarkerWith2xxStatus_HighConfidence`, `TestSSRFInternalTarget_StatusDivergesNoMarker_LowConfidence`, `TestSSRFInternalTarget_IdenticalNon200AcrossPayloads_Suppressed`, `TestSSRFBodyParamTarget_NonOKStatusReflection_Hit` (`tests/unit/detector_ssrf_test.go`); all pre-existing ssrf tests re-verified passing unmodified. `go build`/`go vet`/`go vet -tags eval`/`golangci-lint`: clean. `go test ./... -race`: clean except the pre-existing, unrelated LT-181 `pkg/scriptexec` failure. **Not done:** the built-in candidate address list still has nothing that reaches crAPI's actual internal service mesh (`crapi-web`, confirmed reachable by hand) — hardcoding it would violate CLAUDE.md's no-lab-specific-literal rule, so this stays a real, if narrower, gap specific to this lab's docker-compose topology, not something today's fix (correctly) tries to solve. **Also added:** `--ssrf-body-param`/`--ssrf-body-fill-field`/`--allow-ssrf-body-fill` on `scan` (`cmd/hackerfive/scan.go`) — `pkg/scanner/config.go`/`engine.go` already carried `SSRFBodyParams`/`SSRFBodyFillFields`/`AllowSSRFBodyFill` end to end (LT-96/LT-188 a), but only `agent` ever set them; there was no way to manually exercise the body-field check via the real CLI at all, which is how this fix's live verification was actually run (`hackerfive scan --detector ssrf --ssrf-body-param mechanic_api --templates <empty>` against the real container, isolated from the nuclei corpus) — same 4 findings, same confidences, as the standalone-program check that found and fixed the false-positive class above. `tests/fixtures/known-vulns/crapi.json`'s `crapi-ssrf-contact-mechanic` entry moves from `missed` to `found`.
- **LT-189 (route discovery from a documentation page built 2026-09-21; the depth default is an open decision) — recon never fetched an API's served documentation page, and `agent`'s default recon depth skips the wave that would.** Found by measuring a second lab: on vAPI, recon saw 1 endpoint and all three known vulnerabilities were lost at stage 0. The front page only mentions its docs URL in prose, and the docs page (Redoc) spells out 21 routes with methods in its link anchors. **Built (`pkg/recon/docroutes.go`):** per seed, GET the root and `/docs`, `/redoc`, `/api-docs`, `/documentation`, plus any same-hostname URL the root page mentions (no port or the seed's own; a different host or port is another origin and never followed); read `#tag/<Tag>/paths/<~1-escaped path>/<method>` anchors into `docs-anchor` endpoints with their documented methods; at most 6 pages and 200 routes, scope-gated, rate-limited, circuit-broken. The LT-76 status probe now also covers `docs-anchor` routes (skipping templated ones) and keeps a documented method instead of overwriting it with GET, so a documented route that answers an anonymous 401/403 becomes an authbypass protected path. Live on vAPI at full depth: 1 to 23 endpoints, BOLA found, 0 of 3 to 1 of 3 (doc94 "A second lab"). **Limits:** Redoc-style anchors only; a Swagger UI page (`#/tag/operationId`) carries no paths and would need the spec it loads; a docs page that is only client-rendered from a spec URL is not read (a `spec-url`/`redoc.init(url)` reference would be the next step); vAPI's `serversurfer` and `jwt/user` are in no served evidence at all. **Open decision, not changed:** `hackerfive agent --recon-depth` defaults to `active`, which skips Wave 3 (crawl, JS analysis, shapes, docs routes, seeds, protected paths from bundles): every result in doc94 that found more than the misconfig baseline ran at `full`, the web UI already forces full, and the MCP `plan` defaults to `active`. Making `full` the agent default is the honest default for a mode whose value is depth, at the price of a longer, noisier recon (a katana crawl, up to 300 join probes, 25 shape probes, a few doc-page GETs), so it wants your yes; until then the flag's help text is the only warning.
**Status 2026-09-21:** LT-168 fixed; LT-172 and LT-171 implemented and live-verified (see their entries); LT-173 is still the run-killer — the crAPI re-run above died at turn 6 exactly as vAPI did before (`NextAction` context deadline after 4 min, exit 1, 5 model turns + $0.015 spent, 19 findings kept). With the fast lane it is now rarer (fewer model calls) but not gone. Do LT-173 next. **Suggested order (value ÷ effort):** LT-168 first (a regression that silently breaks recon and the eval harness; small), then LT-172+LT-171 together (the real agent-quality lever; medium-large), LT-173 (small-medium, removes the run-killer), LT-166 + LT-174 (correctness of what a `done` leaf means), then LT-175/170/176/178/177 opportunistically. LT-169 needs its own design pass. Re-run this exact protocol after LT-168+172+171 land and log it as a new dated entry against the [doc93](93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md) M5 baseline.

### From "Live Testing — G1 agent-driven eval (2026-09-10)"

- LT-137 ✅ done 2026-09-10 — `plan` execution never applied doc15 Step 6a's tech-based template narrowing at all (`DerivedTags`/`Tags` left empty in both `tools_plan.go`'s `buildBaseExecConfig` and webui's plan-exec path), so every leaf ran the *entire* synced corpus — a first G1 run timed out on 3/4 scenarios from one misconfig leaf's 3+-minute corpus pass alone. Fixed: both paths now set `DerivedTags`, `planexec.executor.go`'s `runLeaf` unions each leaf's own detector-tag floor on top (mirroring `cmd/hackerfive/scan.go`'s existing `unionScanTags` composition). **Residual, honest limitation:** misconfig's floor tags are broad category words matching most of the corpus, so floor-only narrowing (no tech fingerprint) only trims ~25% — matches the CLI/webui paths' own long-documented behavior.
- **LT-138 (item 1 ✅ fixed 2026-09-19 for `pkg/orchestrator`; item 2 ✅ resolved 2026-09-20 as a stale fixture, not an agent bug — see LT-177)** — two agent-specific gaps surfaced against this item's original 2026-09-10 MCP-plan-mode observation, neither fixed at the time:
  1. crAPI's idor/authbypass leaves resolved *pending* but were *skipped at execution* (missing `--endpoint`/`--protected-paths`) under `--recon-depth active` (no Wave-3 crawl); I4's field-miss resolution never ran — the write-up hedged that `llmfallback.ResolveTreeLeaves`'s field-suggestion path "appears scoped" to an I3-*unresolved* leaf, not a *pending*-but-execution-blocked one, without pinning down the exact function (`ResolveTreeLeaves` is actually I4's *first* caller — leaf-shape resolution for a `StatusUnresolved` leaf — a different function from `pkg/mcpserver/tools_plan.go`'s `resolveFieldSuggestions`/`ResolveFieldMiss`, I4's *second* caller, which already existed and already called I4 for this exact 0-candidate case in MCP-plan mode — see `applyFieldSuggestion`'s own doc comment: an LLM-resolved field miss is deliberately "surfaced ... but never auto-injected into execution," so MCP-plan mode's behavior here was arguably already correct-by-design, not a bug, pending human review between `plan` and `execute`). **The real, unambiguous gap was `pkg/orchestrator`'s own `hackerfive agent` loop** (didn't exist yet when this item was filed): unlike MCP-plan mode, it had *no* field-miss handling at all — a `scan.leaf` dispatch blocked on a required field just skipped permanently, with no human turn in between to notice or resolve it. **Fixed:** `dispatchScanLeaf` (`pkg/orchestrator/orchestrator.go`) now detects a real (non-"excluded by operator") skip on a `fieldMissDetectors` leaf (`idor`/`authbypass`/`ssrf` — the set `fieldsuggest.Deterministic` covers) and calls new `resolveMissingLeafField`: a fresh, targeted recon probe of the leaf's own host, run through the exact same `fieldsuggest.Deterministic` `pkg/mcpserver`/`pkg/webui` already use. A single deterministic candidate auto-fills a copy of `cfg.BaseScanConfig` (no LLM call, no new gate — already unconditional in mcpserver/webui) and the dispatch is retried once, immediately, in the same turn. A genuine miss (0 or >1 candidates) is resolved via I4 (`cfg.Client.ResolveField`, a new method on the orchestrator's `LLMClient` interface, cost tracked against the tree's own spend ceiling) but — matching `applyFieldSuggestion`'s exact precedent — is only ever surfaced in the turn's result summary, never auto-applied: auto-applying a model's own guess at which endpoint to enumerate would fire real, consequential requests based purely on that guess with no human review, the exact gate doc90 Decision 5/6 requires before anything consequential runs. Tests: `pkg/orchestrator/fieldmiss_test.go` (deterministic single-candidate auto-fill and apply, genuine-miss I4 resolution never auto-applied plus its cost tracked, I4 escalation, a failed recon probe surfaced as a note, an end-to-end `dispatch`-level retry against a real HTTP server that ends `StatusDone`, and a non-`fieldMissDetectors` detector's skip passing through completely unchanged with zero extra recon calls).
  2. ✅ **resolved 2026-09-20 — the premise below is stale (LT-177): the deterministic CLI path also no longer emits `misconfig-method-*` against DVWA/Juice Shop (LT-69/73's anti-FP rule), so nothing agent-specific was ever wrong; the expected-findings fixtures need re-baselining.** Original write-up: the native `misconfig-method-*` check (`checkDisallowedMethods`) never fired against DVWA/Juice Shop/vAPI through the agent path despite firing in the CLI baseline against the same targets, identical `Concurrency`/`RateLimit`/`Timeout` defaults ruled out as cause; root cause not yet found — needs a follow-up live session with dedicated time (no Docker/lab-target access was available to investigate this via this pass).

### From "Live Testing — pre-v0.7.0-tag re-verification against www.aalberts.com (2026-09-10)"

- LT-139 ✅ done 2026-09-10 — `misconfig-cors` conflated a literal wildcard `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *` with genuine origin-reflection, both rated high/high — but a literal `*` alongside `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials: true` is spec-non-functional in every standards-compliant browser (Fetch/CORS spec), so the finding overstated severity for aalberts.com's actual (non-reflecting) case. Fixed: `checkCORS` now splits `echoedProbe` (genuine reflection, stays high/high) from `literalWildcard` (down-ranked to low/medium, description corrected). Test: `TestMisconfigCORS_LiteralWildcardWithCredentials_DownRanked`.
- LT-139b ✅ done 2026-09-10 — `SuggestedAction.Detail map[string]string` couldn't decode a real model response (`suggest --llm-assist` correctly proposed a `triage_group` action with an array-valued `finding_ids` field), so `decodeJSONResponse` failed the *entire* response and silently degraded to ledger-only — a good, safe proposal thrown away per the "degrade, never fabricate" contract, but a real capability loss. Fixed: widened `Detail` to `map[string]any`; prompt documents the array shape. Test: `TestSuggest_TriageGroupWithFindingIDsList_Decodes`.

### From "Live Testing — nettix.com.pe full-pipeline round (2026-09-11)"

- **LT-144 (fixed 2026-09-11)** — `runJSStaticAnalysis` (`pkg/recon/jsstatic.go:313-325`) calls `agg.addEndpoint` for every `extractJSEndpoints` hit with **no `r.scope.Allowed(...)` check** — its own sibling function 15 lines above, `recordCloudBucketRef` (`jsstatic.go:298-306`), scope-checks for exactly this reason ("a page can reference any number of third-party buckets that have nothing to do with the target's own infrastructure") but the fix was never applied to the plain-endpoint path. Live-reproduced: a crawled page's `xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml"` (XHTML namespace declaration, not a link) became `EndpointFact{URL: "http://www.w3.org/1999/xhtml", Source: "js-static"}` — `extractJSEndpoints`'s absolute-URL branch (`jsstatic.go:109-120`) only checks `IsPlausibleURLPath`/`IsNonRouteAssetPath` (path *shape*, not host/scope). `SuggestIDOREndpointCandidates` (`suggest.go:160`) then templated it into `/{{id}}/xhtml`, and `registry.Resolve` (which trusts recon already scope-filtered, per its own doc comment) built a real, dispatchable `idor` leaf: `{Target: "http://www.w3.org", EndpointTemplate: "/{{id}}/xhtml"}` in the live PlanTree (`plan.json`'s `host:www.w3.org` node). Approved via Plan Preview (default-checked leaves) or driven through MCP, this fires real enumeration requests at an entirely unrelated third-party domain — a direct violation of CLAUDE.md's scope rule. **Fix:** add the identical scope check `recordCloudBucketRef` already has, right before `runJSStaticAnalysis`'s `agg.addEndpoint` call; on rejection, `agg.addOutOfScope(hostOnly(epURL))` like every other out-of-scope path in this codebase, so it's loud, not silent. **Applied**: `runJSStaticAnalysis` (`jsstatic.go:313-341`) now checks `r.scope.Allowed(epURL)` before `agg.addEndpoint`, calling `agg.addOutOfScope(hostOnly(epURL))` on rejection — same shape as `recordCloudBucketRef`. Regression test `TestRunWave3_JSStaticAnalysis_OutOfScopeAbsoluteEndpoint_NotDispatched` (`jsstatic_test.go`) reproduces the exact live `xmlns`/www.w3.org case and asserts the URL never reaches `result.Endpoints` and its host lands in `result.OutOfScope` instead. Full suite (`go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -race`) and `golangci-lint` clean.
- **LT-145 (fixed 2026-09-11)** — naabu found port **10000 (Webmin) open on 8 of nettix's 24 hosts** this round, and a first-party `checkWebmin` check already exists (`pkg/detectors/misconfig/detector.go:1223`) — but it's structurally unreachable: `interestingPorts`/`netserviceCheckedPorts` (`pkg/registry/decisionengine.go:218-226,240`) cover only 7 raw-TCP-protocol ports (ftp/telnet/mysql/postgresql/redis/elasticsearch/mongodb), so port 10000 gets **no leaf at all**, not even a visibility one; and even if it did, `resolveTechFact`'s misconfig leaf always targets the host's default HTTP(S) port (`reconHostBaseURL`), never `:10000`, so `checkWebmin`'s `doRequest(..., target, ..., "/", ...)` (detector.go:1224) would hit the wrong port anyway. Same gap plausibly applies to any other HTTP-on-a-custom-port admin panel. **Fix (simplest):** mirror `checkDolibarrOutdated`/`checkNextcloudStatus`'s existing "always try me, unconditionally" posture — have `checkWebmin` also probe `https://host:10000/` directly, self-contained, no registry/PlanTree plumbing needed (same pattern these already use to bypass the corpus-time-budget problem, LT-98). A more general fix (thread a "known HTTP admin port" table through `resolvePortFacts` into a misconfig leaf carrying the right port) would also cover a future product without a bespoke self-probe. **Applied**: `checkWebmin` (`detector.go`) now probes twice — its leaf's own target as before (unchanged, the rare reverse-proxied-onto-default-port case), plus an unconditional second probe at `webminPortTarget`'s rebuilt `<host>:10000` address, same scheme as the original target. Factored the original single-address logic into `checkWebminAt`; both probes' findings are merged. The `resolvePortFacts`/registry-plumbing alternative was not pursued — this self-probe needed no other file changed, consistent with `checkDolibarrOutdated`/`checkNextcloudStatus`'s existing posture. Regression test `TestMisconfigWebmin_AdminPort10000_ProbedEvenWhenMainSiteIsSomethingElse` (`tests/unit/detector_misconfig_test.go`) binds a real listener on `127.0.0.1:10000` serving Webmin while the main target serves unrelated content, and asserts the finding still surfaces (skips itself if port 10000 is unavailable in the test environment). Known limitation, not new: a self-signed cert on the `:10000` probe fails without `--insecure`, same pre-existing constraint every other HTTPS probe in this detector already has.
- **LT-146 (fixed 2026-09-11)** — `llmfallback`'s shared HTTP timeout (`requestTimeout = 240 * time.Second`, `client.go:50`, already raised from 60s→180s→240s over LT-25's history) still isn't enough: **2 of 6 real LLM calls timed out** in one `plan --llm-assist` run against the configured `deepseek/deepseek-v4-flash-0731` model — `ResolveLeaf` for `www.nettix.com.pe-leaf-10` ("reading response: context deadline exceeded") and the `VetPendingLeaves` plausibility-review call (same error). Total wall-clock for the LLM-assist phase was ~14 minutes for what should be ~6 calls; total spend was $0.0061 of the $0.10 ceiling, so cost wasn't the constraint, latency reliability was. Compounding this: **zero mid-call visibility** — the verbose log only prints on a leaf's completion/failure (`resolve.go`), so a slow/hung single-shot call (`PlanFromRecon`, `VetPendingLeaves` — both un-batched, one call for the whole tree) gives no "still waiting on X" signal, indistinguishable from a genuine hang. **Fix ideas:** a heartbeat log line when a call has been outstanding past some threshold (e.g. 30s); consider giving the single-shot whole-tree calls (`PlanFromRecon`, `VetPendingLeaves`) a *longer* timeout than the per-leaf ones, since they carry more input and currently share the one constant; or add one bounded retry for a bare timeout (not a real API error) before giving up. **Applied**: all three fix ideas landed. (1) `Client` gained a `WithLogCallback`/`logCB` seam (same shape as `idor`/`misconfig`'s existing one) wired into every frontend with a log sink today — CLI's `plan`/`triage`/`suggest` (to `cmd.ErrOrStderr()`) and webui's Plan Preview resolve + scan-launch field-fill (to `Job.AppendLog`); MCP's plan/triage tools have no log sink of their own yet (LT-131) and simply omit it, a no-op since `logCB` is nil-safe. (2) `requestTimeout` (240s) now covers only per-leaf/per-field calls (`ResolveLeaf` + its draft-template follow-up, `ResolveField`); a new `requestTimeoutLong` (420s) covers every single-shot whole-dataset call — not just the two that timed out live, but every call of that same shape (`PlanFromRecon`, `VetPendingLeaves`, `TriageFindings`, `Suggest`), since they'd hit the same wall on a large enough scan. (3) `completeLabeled`/`completeOnce` (`client.go`) wrap every real call in its own per-call `context.WithTimeout` (the shared `http.Client.Timeout` is now fixed at `requestTimeoutLong` purely as an outer backstop, never the binding constraint), a periodic heartbeat logged every `heartbeatInterval` (30s, a `var` for test control) naming the call (`"ResolveLeaf <leaf-id>"`, `"PlanFromRecon"`, etc.), and exactly one retry when the failure was specifically *this* per-call deadline firing (`isOwnTimeout` — distinguished from the caller's own outer ctx being canceled/expired, which gets no retry, and from a real API/decode error, which also gets no retry). Tests: `pkg/llmfallback/reliability_test.go` (7 cases covering the retry/no-retry/heartbeat/Option-wiring behavior).
- **LT-147 (fixed 2026-09-11)** — `aggregator.addHost` (`pkg/recon/aggregate.go`, right after `addEndpoint`) appends unconditionally, unlike `addTech`'s `NormalizeHost`-keyed merge (LT-14). Live-reproduced: the apex target itself (`nettix.com.pe`) got two separate `HostFact` entries in one recon run — one from Wave 1's ASN/WHOIS lookup (`Source: "passive-whois-asn"`, carries `.notes`), one from Wave 2's httpx/naabu probe (`Source: "httpx"`, carries `.ports`) — every other one of the 24 discovered hosts got exactly one entry. Cosmetic today (a split-looking row in the Web UI's Hosts table) but the same class of bug LT-14 fixed for `TechFact`. **Fix:** give `addHost` the same `NormalizeHost`-keyed merge (union `.notes`/`.ports`/`.headers` fields rather than dropping the second entry). **Applied**: `addHost` now mirrors `addTech`'s shape exactly — a `NormalizeHost`-keyed index (`hostIndex`/`hostSources`), comma-joined `Source`, best-`Confidence`-wins — but unions `Notes` (append) and `Ports` (via a new `mergePortFacts` helper, so a port both waves happened to observe isn't duplicated) instead of merging a `Name` field (`HostFact` has none). No `Headers` field exists on `HostFact` to union — the fix's original note overstated that part. Tests: `TestAddHost_SameHost_MergesInsteadOfDuplicating` (reproduces the exact live passive-whois-asn/httpx pairing), `TestAddHost_WwwAndCaseHostVariants_MergeInsteadOfDuplicating`, `TestAddHost_DifferentHost_StaysDistinct`, `TestAddHost_SameSourceTwice_SourceNotDuplicatedInString`, `TestAddHost_LowerConfidenceArrivesSecond_DoesNotDowngrade`, `TestAddHost_DuplicatePort_NotDuplicated` (`pkg/recon/aggregate_test.go`).
- **LT-148 (half-fixed 2026-09-11 — the LT-6-tail duplicate-finding symptom; the PlanTree-honesty half is still open)** — `registry.Resolve` builds full per-host template leaves with no awareness of D6's uniform-response-wall classification (`ReconResult.UniformResponses`, LT-140) at all — that's purely a *scan-time* short-circuit (`pkg/scanner/uniformwall.go`). Live-reproduced: the PlanTree from a target recon already flagged 100%-WAF-blocked (`ns1`/`web01`/`web02`/`sinchi01`, canary status 401) still carries 8-9 "will run this CVE/admin-panel check" leaves each, indistinguishable in Plan Preview from a leaf that will actually fire. Worse, `planexec.runLeaf` (`executor.go:442,550`) dispatches each leaf through its own fresh `scanner.Engine.Run` call — so uwGate's per-*target* check (`engine.go:328`) re-evaluates once per *leaf* on a walled host, producing N duplicate `misconfig-waf-blocked`/`misconfig-uniform-catchall` findings when driven through webui/mcp (the CLI `scan --recon-file` path batches all of a host's dispatch into one `Engine.Run`, so it doesn't show this). Ties directly to the already-known, still-open LT-6 tail ("webui/mcp never call `Dedup`/`SplitAggregates`") — Dedup would collapse the duplicates if it ran there. **Fix ideas:** have `Resolve` itself drop/demote a host's template leaves (keep the built-in-detector leaf only) when `result.UniformResponseForHost(host)` is non-nil, so Plan Preview shows the truth up front; separately, either call `Dedup` on webui/mcp's own accumulated findings (closing LT-6's tail) or have `planexec.RunPlan` batch a host's leaves into one `Engine.Run` invocation. **Applied (the LT-6-tail half)**: `planexec.RunPlan` (`executor.go`) now runs its final accumulated `findings` through the identical `reporter.SplitAggregates` → `reporter.DropSupersededNucleiFindings` → `reporter.Dedup` pipeline `cmd/hackerfive/scan.go:354` already ran for the CLI path — closing LT-6's tail for both of `RunPlan`'s callers (webui, mcp) in one place, guarded to a no-op when `findings` is empty so the function's nil-return contract for an empty/fully-skipped plan is unchanged. `opts.OnFinding`'s live per-leaf streaming callback is untouched (still raw, pre-Dedup) — only the final batch `RunPlan` returns is deduped. Regression test `TestRunPlan_UniformWallFinding_DedupedAcrossLeaves` (`executor_test.go`) reproduces two same-host leaves each independently triggering the D6 short-circuit and asserts exactly one `misconfig-waf-blocked` finding survives. **Still open**: `registry.Resolve` itself still builds the misleading leaves in the first place — Plan Preview still shows 8-9 "will run" rows for a walled host before any dispatch happens; only the post-dispatch finding list is now honest. That half (dropping/demoting template leaves at PlanTree-build time when `result.UniformResponseForHost(host)` is non-nil) is unaddressed.
- **LT-149 (fixed 2026-09-11)** — neither `recon`/`plan --verbose` nor `scan`'s template-dispatch phase print any progress timestamp or heartbeat. `verboseProgress` (`cmd/hackerfive/recon.go:170-174`) is exactly `"%s: %s\n", wave, status` — no `time.Now()` — so a `wave3: running` → `wave3: done` pair gives no idea which wave is slow or whether a wave's own internal cap fired (this run: `naabu`/`katana` each silently hit their 2-minute wave-timeout with partial results, only visible after the fact via the warnings array, not the progress log). `scanner.Engine`'s corpus dispatch (`engine.go`) is worse: **zero output between the "loaded N templates" line and the final "scan finished in Xm" summary**, however many minutes that spans (this run: a `--max-target-duration 15m` scan against 6 targets gave no signal at all for 15 straight minutes). **Fix:** add elapsed-time to `verboseProgress`'s two lines; add a periodic ("dispatched N/M templates for target X so far") heartbeat to `Engine.Run`'s corpus loop, mirroring `--verbose`'s existing posture elsewhere in this codebase (LT-11/LT-37's precedent). **Applied**: `verboseProgress` now prefixes each line with `[+<elapsed>]` since the callback itself was registered (`verboseProgressNow`, an indirected `time.Now` so a test can drive it deterministically instead of racing a real clock). `runTemplates` (`engine.go`) gained a periodic heartbeat, factored into a standalone `startDispatchHeartbeat` (new `heartbeat.go`) for testability: every `dispatchHeartbeatInterval` (30s, a `var` so a test can shrink it) it logs `"<target>: <completed>/<total> template(s) completed so far"` via the existing `e.warnf` seam, tracking completions with an atomic counter (`fire`'s many goroutines each increment it) against the same `len(nativeTemplates)+len(nucleiTemplates)` total the existing time-budget/adaptive-abort findings already report. `stop()` blocks until the heartbeat goroutine has actually exited, so `runTemplates`' deferred call is guaranteed to leave nothing running past its own return. Tests: `TestVerboseProgress_WritesWaveStatusToStderr` (updated for the new format), `TestStartDispatchHeartbeat_LogsProgressUntilStopped`, `TestStartDispatchHeartbeat_ZeroTotal_NeverLogs` (`pkg/scanner/heartbeat_test.go`).
- **LT-151 (logging fix applied 2026-09-11; root-cause still needs a live re-scan to confirm)** — `cloud01.nettix.com.pe` and `cloud02.nettix.com.pe` serve byte-identical `/status.php` (confirmed live via direct `curl`: both `{"version":"28.0.5.1",...}`), yet this round's scan produced `misconfig-nextcloud-status-disclosure` + `misconfig-nextcloud-outdated` for `cloud02` and **zero findings of any kind** (not even missing-security-header ones) for `cloud01` — reproducing the exact symptom LT-113 (2026-09-08) was supposed to have fixed ("cloud02 got its Nextcloud findings but cloud01, identical, got 0"). No warning/error logged for `cloud01` anywhere in `scan-misconfig.stderr`. Leading hypothesis, not confirmed: every native product check (`checkDolibarrOutdated`, `checkNextcloudStatus`, `checkWebmin`, `checkWPUserEnum`, ...) treats *any* `doRequest` error as a silent `return nil, nil` — "not this product," indistinguishable from a genuine transient network hiccup (this same session independently observed `www.nettix.com.pe` returning one transient `502` mid-run). If `cloud01`'s very first priority-check request hit a blip, the finding is gone with zero trace. **Fix (if confirmed):** have the priority-check tier log (at `warn`/`info`, not silently) when `doRequest` itself errors, distinguishing "no request could be sent" from "product signature didn't match" — cheap, and would have made this round's asymmetry immediately diagnosable instead of requiring an independent live `curl` to even notice. **Applied**: `misconfig.Detector` gained the same `WithLogCallback`/`logFn` seam `idor.Detector` already had; all five priority checks (`checkWPUserEnum`, `checkDolibarrOutdated`, `checkNextcloudStatus`, `checkPhpMyAdmin`, `checkWebmin`) now call `d.logPriorityCheckRequestErr(check, target, err)` — a `warn`-level line — instead of silently returning `nil, nil` on a `doRequest` failure; `pkg/scanner/engine.go`'s `runDetector` wires it to `e.warnf`, same as `idor`'s existing callback. Regression test `TestMisconfigPriorityCheck_RequestErr_LoggedNotSilent` (`tests/unit/detector_misconfig_test.go`) points the detector at a closed listener and asserts at least one `warn:` line naming the failing check. This closes the *symptom* (silent failures are now loud) but the specific cloud01/cloud02 asymmetry's root cause is still unconfirmed — a future live re-scan against nettix.com.pe with this fix in place would show a warn line for cloud01 if the hypothesis is right, or rule it out if not.

### From "Live Testing — nettix.com.pe Part A/B verification (2026-09-10)"

- **LT-124 ✅ done 2026-09-10** — every authbypass check blindly trusted `resp.StatusCode` after the shared client transparently follows a redirect — a live FALSE POSITIVE: `GET /wp-admin/` (302→`wp-login.php`, correctly protected) was reported as `authbypass-missing-auth-wp-admin` high/high because the client followed the redirect and graded the login page's 200. Fix: `redirectedAwayFrom(req, resp)` gates all five authbypass checks (porting `misconfig`'s existing pattern). idor/ssrf's identical blind-status pattern left as-is (self-corrects via baseline/diff).
- **LT-125 ✅ done 2026-09-10** — `SuggestAuthBypassPathsFromRecon` only recognized 401/403 as "protected path" evidence, missing the extremely common "3xx redirect to a login page" shape — which is *why* this round had zero authbypass leaves despite `/wp-admin/` sitting right there as a recon-observed `302`. Fix: `redirectsToLoginBoundary` bucket ahead of the other cases. Landed with LT-124, sequenced after it.

### From the 2026-09-08 baseline-run / apex-rerun subsections (items added 2026-09-11 through 2026-09-23)

- **LT-140 ✅ done 2026-09-11** — `ReconResult.UniformResponse` was one fact, not one per host, so D6's corpus-skip (LT-59) only ever protected a single host per multi-host recon run — `aggregator.setUniformResponse` kept only "the first verdict seen," and all three frontends built `UniformWallHosts` (already a `map[string]string`) from that one fact. **Fix:** `UniformResponseFact` widened to a per-host collection — `ReconResult.UniformResponses []UniformResponseFact` (schema v1.13, a deliberately breaking rename: `uniform_response` → `uniform_responses`, agreed since no real external MCP consumer depended on the old shape yet); `aggregator.uniformResponses` keyed by `NormalizeHost` (per-host first-writer-wins, same convention as `addTech`'s LT-14 dedup); `classifyAppSurface` now judges "blind" against every walled host, not one named host; new `ReconResult.UniformWallHosts()` / `UniformResponseForHost(host)` helpers replace each of the 4 call sites' (`cmd/hackerfive/scan.go`, `pkg/webui/handlers_launch.go`, `pkg/mcpserver/tools_plan.go`/`tools_scan.go`) hand-rolled single-entry map. Also fixed two latent cross-host bugs the single-fact shape had baked in: `reconShowsAdminSurface` (LT-58) and `hostServesDynamicContent` (LT-67) in `pkg/registry/decisionengine.go` now resolve the wall verdict per endpoint/leaf host — before, a WAF wall on host A could blanket-suppress a real 401/403 admin signal or secret-grep leaf on unrelated host B in the same multi-host run. → [Phase 8](17-implementation-plan-ph8.md) Step 5 DoD (filed alongside LT-64/65/84's per-host fact attribution theme, not Step 6 as originally noted here — Step 6 is Eval Maturity + Release, a stale reference from before this doc's 2026-09-10 step renumbering).
- **LT-157 (✅ fixed 2026-09-19)** — evidence-gate LLM-asserted severity/confidence in `llmfallback.TriageFindings`/`Suggest`: don't let a triage response upgrade a finding's severity/confidence unless the specific `Finding.Evidence` field it cites actually contains the marker the claim depends on (a deterministic post-check, not another LLM call). Concrete motivating bug: the `misconfig-cors` detector's own overclaimed description got parroted verbatim into a triage rationale for a literal-wildcard CORS response that isn't actually exploitable without also satisfying the credentials rule — neither the detector nor triage caught it. Cross-project research (2026-09-14, see [discussions.md](discussions.md) if logged) backs this up from two directions: Strix's benchmarked design only ships a finding when a real PoC fires against it ("if the exploit doesn't fire, the finding doesn't ship"), while Cyber-AutoAgent's unverified self-reported `[CONFIDENCE] 95%` (the model grading its own claim, no independent check) is the failure mode to avoid. **Fixed:** since neither `RankedFinding.Rationale` nor `SuggestedAction.Description` can structurally mutate a `Finding`'s `Severity`/`Confidence` at all (both are already free-text-only per `types.go`'s doc comments — doc02's "detector-set, never agent-writable" invariant already held), the real gap was that free text; a triage/suggest rationale could still *assert* certainty a finding's own fields don't support. New `pkg/llmfallback/evidencegate.go`'s `evidenceGateText` is the deterministic post-check: a short, hand-curated, case-insensitive list of unhedged certainty phrases ("fully exploitable", "guaranteed", "grants full access", ...) is checked against the cited `Finding`'s own `Confidence`/`Severity` — the only structurally reliable evidence-quality signal available without spending another LLM call to verify the claim (a full semantic grounding check against arbitrary `Evidence` text would need either another model call or a fragile per-detector keyword→field table). A match on a finding that isn't `Confidence: "high"` and `Severity` in `{"high","critical"}` gets a `[unverified claim — ...]` caveat prepended — the model's own text is never rewritten or dropped, only flagged. Wired into both `TriageFindings` (gates each `RankedFinding.Rationale` against its own finding) and `Suggest` (gates `run_leaf`/`triage_group` actions' `Description` against `weakestFinding` of whatever `finding_id`/`finding_ids` the action's `Detail` names — actions with no finding reference, like `draft_template`/`redo_recon`, are left untouched since there's nothing to gate against). Tests: `pkg/llmfallback/evidencegate_test.go` (phrase detection, strong-vs-weak-finding gating, zero-value/empty-text no-ops, `weakestFinding` tie-breaking, `findingsFromDetail` single/list/unknown-id extraction) plus integration tests in `TestTriageFindings_OverclaimingRationaleOnWeakFinding_GetsCaveated`/`TestSuggest_RunLeafOverclaimingReferencedFinding_GetsCaveated`/`TestSuggest_ActionWithNoFindingReference_DescriptionUnaffected`.
- **LT-160 (✅ both items fixed — item 2 closed 2026-09-19)** — `pkg/orchestrator`'s M2 loop ([93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md](93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md)) had two deliberate scope-narrowings against the milestone's original sketch, both safe (fail-closed, never fabricate) but real gaps to close before M5's lab-target validation:
  1. ✅ fixed 2026-09-19 — `script.explore`'s evidence-reconfirmation pipeline (M1's "independently re-issue the discovered request through `pkg/scanner/httpclient` before packaging it as `Finding.Evidence`", closing LT-157's self-reported-confidence flaw) wasn't built — a script's stdout was only surfaced in turn history for a human/future turn to act on, with no code path turning it into a `Finding` at all. Fixed by new `pkg/orchestrator/scriptevidence.go`: `buildCatalog`'s `script.explore` tool description now tells the model to print one `HACKERFIVE_FINDING_CANDIDATE: {json}` line per candidate, naming a request and a falsifiable `confirm_status`/`confirm_contains` condition. `dispatchScriptExplore` hands the sandbox's stdout to `reconfirmScriptCandidates`, which independently re-issues each candidate's exact request via `pkg/scanner/httpclient` (never trusting the sandbox's self-reported exit code/stdout) and only ships a `Finding` — `Confidence: "high"`, `Evidence["confirmation_method"]` naming the re-issue — when the real re-issued response actually matches the candidate's own stated condition. A candidate with neither confirm field, one whose re-issued response doesn't match, one outside `cfg.BaseScanConfig.Scope`/the seed target's host, or a mutating method without `--allow-writes` (reusing the scan's existing gate, not a new one) is dropped and logged, never silently promoted. Covered by `pkg/orchestrator/scriptevidence_test.go` (confirmed-by-status, confirmed-by-substring, not-confirmed-on-mismatch, no-confirm-condition, out-of-scope, real-scope-file, mutating-method-gate, and stdout-parsing tests).
  2. ✅ fixed 2026-09-19 — `recon.refresh`'s result wasn't merged back into the running `PlanTree` (no leaf-shape-changing merge existed post-construction outside `registry.Resolve`'s own initial build) — it only returned a fact-count summary into turn history, so a mid-run recon refresh couldn't surface a genuinely new leaf the model could act on next turn. **Fixed:** new `mergeReconRefresh` (`pkg/orchestrator/orchestrator.go`) runs `dispatchReconRefresh`'s fresh `*recon.ReconResult` through the exact same deterministic `registry.Resolve` the initial tree build already uses — so a "new leaf" is never an LLM inventing one, only the same rule table re-applied to newly observed facts, the same additive-only pattern `llmfallback.MergeLLMProposals` already established for an LLM-proposed leaf. A host the fresh recon discovered that the tree never had at all gets its whole freshly-resolved subtree attached under root; a host the tree already has gets only its genuinely new leaves (deduped by a new shared `leafDispatchKey{Detector, Target, EndpointTemplate}` — also now used by the pre-existing `warnDuplicateLeafTargets`, so the two never drift into checking different notions of "identical leaf") attached via the existing `agenttask.AttachLeaf`; every existing leaf's own `Status`/`Confidence`/`Rationale` is left untouched — doc02 Design Principle 5 ("a later pass can only ever weaken a plan, never strengthen it... none can add a leaf") constrains leaf-*resolution* passes re-judging an existing leaf, which this isn't: it's growing the tree from new deterministic facts, the same operation `registry.Resolve`'s initial build already performs. The `recon.refresh` tool's own catalog description (`buildCatalog`) was updated to stop claiming it "does not change the plan tree's shape." Tests: `pkg/orchestrator/reconrefresh_test.go` (nil-input no-ops, brand-new-host subtree attachment, existing-host genuinely-new-leaves-only merge with a re-merge idempotency check, and an end-to-end `Run`-level test proving a `recon.refresh`-discovered leaf becomes immediately dispatchable via a later `scan.leaf` call in the same run).
- **LT-161 (✅ fixed 2026-09-19)** — `pkg/webui`'s Plan Preview execute flow (`handlers_plan_exec.go`'s `executePlan`, dispatching `planexec.RunPlan`) never set `Job.execCfg.Scope` — only `.ScopeFile` (a bare path). `RunPlan`'s B4 scope-creep gate (`executor.go`, `baseCfg.Scope != nil`) reads `.Scope` directly with no `ScopeFile` fallback of its own (only `scanner.Engine.loadScope` has that fallback, and `RunPlan` never goes through `Engine`) — so a plan-execution leaf dispatched from the Web UI ran with B4's scope-creep gate silently inert, regardless of whatever `--scope`/`scope_file` the operator entered at launch. Found during [93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md](93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md) M4 research (2026-09-15) while confirming how `pkg/orchestrator`'s own `BaseScanConfig.Scope` needed to be wired (M4's `runLaunchAgentJob` parses `form.ScopeFile` into a real `*scope.Scope` itself, sidestepping this gap for the new agent path — but the pre-existing Plan Preview path was untouched). Fixed exactly as scoped: `parseLaunchSubmission` (`handlers_launch.go`) now parses `form.ScopeFile` once via `scope.Parse` right after building `execCfg` and sets `execCfg.Scope` directly — a parse failure is now surfaced as a form-submission error (`h.rerenderLaunchWithErrors`) instead of only failing silently and separately inside `runLaunchRecon` later, with `execCfg.Scope` left nil either way. `cfgs`' own per-detector configs (the checked-tab flow) were left untouched — they go through `scanner.Engine`, whose `loadScope` already has the `ScopeFile` fallback, so they were never actually affected by this gap. Covered by two new tests in `handlers_launch_test.go`: `TestParseLaunchSubmission_ScopeFile_PopulatesExecCfgScope` (a valid scope file populates `execCfg.Scope` and it correctly allows/denies) and `TestParseLaunchSubmission_InvalidScopeFile_FailsAsFormError` (a bad path is a form error, `execCfg.Scope` stays nil).
- **LT-162 ✅ closed 2026-09-20 — gate met** — [93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md](93-implementation-plan-agent-orchestrator.md) M5's three-way FP/FN/cost/wall-clock table, re-run after five more fixes landed this round (evidence-gating overclaimed LLM rationale text — LT-157; `script.explore` reconfirmation and `recon.refresh` tree-merging — both halves of LT-160; the `NextAction` prompt's leaf-list cap — LT-165 item 1; the IDOR query-key dedup fix — LT-166's related bug; and I4 field-miss resolution in `pkg/orchestrator` — LT-138 item 1): **orchestrator mode now beats the deterministic baseline, 13/22 vs. 10/22** (full per-target table in the "Live Testing — G1 three-way eval re-verification, isolated lab targets (2026-09-20)" entry below). DVWA 4/10 (beats 3/10), Juice Shop 2/5 (ties), vAPI 5/5 (beats 4/5 — the only miss, `nuclei-php-detect`, is now found), crAPI 2/2 (beats 1/2 — `nuclei-http-missing-security-headers`, missed by *both* deterministic crAPI scenarios, is found). Per doc93's own standing rule ("do not point this mode at a real program until [this table] exists and shows a genuine improvement"), that condition is now met — **`hackerfive agent` may be considered for a real, appropriately-scoped, human-supervised program**, not lab/CLI-only by default. Two live-testing caveats worth carrying forward rather than treating this as an unconditional green light: (1) this re-run required manually isolating each target (stopping every other lab container) to get a trustworthy number at all — the real scope-model gap this exercise surfaced was tracked and fixed same-day as LT-167 (`Scope` now supports an optional `host:port` scope entry); (2) crAPI's run still hit the harness's 20-minute ceiling before producing a clean final `Result` (`cost`/`Iterations` for that leg are therefore unknown, not zero — LT-166, root cause now precisely diagnosed 2026-09-20, is the reason: every `scan.leaf` dispatch on that host redundantly reloads and refires the full template corpus, including a real crAPI-specific template, rather than testing just that leaf's own endpoint — inflating both turn count and raw finding volume for this row without invalidating the fixture-prefix win itself).
- **LT-163 (✅ all three items fixed — item 3 closed 2026-09-19)** — re-testing LT-162 against vAPI/crAPI surfaced two real gaps in `tests/eval/orchestrator_run.go`/`orchestrator_eval_test.go`, plus one more general robustness gap in `hackerfive agent` itself:
  1. ✅ fixed — `OrchestratorScenario` had no `--templates` override field, unlike every deterministic `Scenario` in `challenges.go` (which explicitly scopes vAPI to `./templates/` and crAPI's IDOR run away from the full corpus). Without it, `hackerfive agent` auto-appended the full ~2,500-tag-scoped synced corpus, and vAPI's already-documented "full corpus hangs against its dev server" gotcha (`docs/20-setup-testing-targets-macos.md`) made a single `scan.leaf` take 4+ minutes — with `Config.MinIterations` forcing several such turns, the run blew the harness's timeout with 0 tool_calls ever completing (an infra artifact, not a reasoning miss). Fixed by adding `OrchestratorScenario.Templates` and wiring `--templates` into the dispatched args; vAPI set to `"./templates/"`, crAPI to `templatesDir()` — both mirroring their deterministic-scenario counterparts. Live-verified: vAPI's orchestrator run went from timeout/0 findings to 4/5 (matching the deterministic baseline exactly).
  2. ✅ fixed — the per-scenario context timeout was a flat 10 minutes, copied from `TestAgentEvalHarness` (MCP-plan mode) before `Config.MinIterations` (LT-162's own fix) existed. Live-verified against crAPI: individual `NextAction` calls against `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` took 60-150s+, and `MinIterations`'s forced floor of dispatched turns (plus any rejected-stop retries, each its own `NextAction` round trip) pushed total wall-clock past 10 minutes on a target with more candidate leaves to consider than vAPI/DVWA/Juice Shop. Raised to 20 minutes in `orchestrator_eval_test.go`.
  3. **✅ fixed 2026-09-19** — found via the same crAPI run: it was killed by the (then-10m) timeout mid-run, after a real `misconfig-exposed-path-.env` (high severity — crAPI's own `.env` file, containing real secrets, is genuinely publicly exposed) had already been surfaced live via `OnLog`/turn history, but `hackerfive agent`'s CLI only printed its final `Result` JSON after `orchestrator.Run` returned cleanly — a killed process (this harness's timeout, but equally a real Ctrl-C, OOM, or host reboot) discarded every finding already discovered that run, with no partial output at all. Fixed by changing `cmd/hackerfive/agent.go`'s stdout/`--output` contract from one pretty-printed `Result` document written only at the end to a JSON-Lines stream (new `agentStreamEvent`/`agentEventWriter`): a `{"type":"finding",...}` line is written and flushed the instant `Config.OnFinding` fires (already called synchronously per real detector match, per `pkg/planexec.ExecOptions.OnFinding`'s own doc comment), and a final `{"type":"result",...}` line carries the complete `orchestrator.Result` once `Run` returns (with `error` set if `Run` itself errored, so a partial-but-real `Result` on a `NextAction` failure is never dropped either). `os.Create`/`os.Stdout` are both unbuffered Go-level writers, so a completed `Encode` call is durable in the OS pipe/file the instant it returns, regardless of what kills the process afterward. `agentEventWriter` serializes concurrent writes (a leaf's scanner engine can report findings from more than one worker goroutine) — covered by `cmd/hackerfive/agent_test.go`'s new `TestAgentEventWriter_*` tests (streaming order, a `Run`-error result event, and a 50-goroutine concurrent-write test asserting every line still parses independently). `tests/eval/orchestrator_eval_test.go` was updated to scan stdout line-by-line and collect `finding` events (falling back to them when a killed run's `result` event never arrives) instead of requiring one whole-buffer `json.Unmarshal` — this is what makes a killed run's real findings actually count in the FP/FN table (LT-166's redispatch issue no longer implies "0 findings observed" the way it did before this fix; see LT-166 for the actual redispatch root cause, still open).
- **LT-164 (✅ fixed 2026-09-19)** — crAPI's orchestrator run found 0/2 expected prefixes (vs. the deterministic baseline's 1/2, LT-163) because recon never discovered `/workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report` on its own (the deterministic `Scenario` is handed it directly via `--endpoint`). Investigated live against the real container before assuming a fix: crAPI does **not** expose a per-service OpenAPI/Swagger spec at any conventional path, authenticated or not (`/identity/`'s Spring gateway 401s *every* path under its prefix, real or not, before route resolution even runs — confirmed by diffing a known-real vs. a made-up path, both 401) — so extending `probeCommonPaths`' spec-walk to retry under discovered prefixes, the originally-proposed fix, would not have helped. The real, live-verified gap: crAPI's React bundle builds its request URLs at runtime from separately-declared string constants (`ig="workshop/"` + `sg.GET_SERVICE_REPORT="api/mechanic/mechanic_report"` + `"?report_id="+id`), and neither piece alone is endpoint-shaped enough for `extractJSEndpoints` (`jsstatic.go`) to ever see it. Fixed by adding a join pass (`extractJSPathJoinParts`/`buildJSJoinPairs`/`verifyJSJoinCandidates`, `pkg/recon/jsstatic.go`) that reconstructs prefix+base(+query-suffix) candidates from a bundle's own constants and live-verifies each one (bounded to `maxJSJoinProbes`) before emitting it as a `js-static-joined` EndpointFact — critically, verification diffs each candidate against a **per-prefix canary** (`fetchJSPrefixCanary`), not just a status-code allow-list, specifically because identity/'s blanket-401 behavior would otherwise "verify" a wrong prefix+base combination just as confidently as the real one, reintroducing the exact multi-candidate ambiguity this step exists to resolve (live-verified: an earlier version without the canary diff produced both `identity/api/mechanic/mechanic_report` and `workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report` as "verified," the canary diff correctly keeps only the real one). Two more real, unrelated bugs surfaced and were fixed along the way, both pre-existing and both silently capping recon on *any* real-world SPA, not just crAPI: (1) `maxJSStaticBodyBytes` truncated a captured JS body at 512 KiB — crAPI's single-file CRA bundle is 1.65 MB with its route constants sitting at byte ~1.6M, entirely past the old cap (raised to 4 MiB); (2) `runKatana`'s JSONL scanner (`crawl.go`) had a 1 MiB `bufio.Scanner` max-token-size — once the JS bundle's body pushed a single JSONL line past it, `Scan()` silently returned false and **the rest of that crawl's output, not just that line, was discarded with no error surfaced**, which is why `main.js` never appeared in `hackerfive recon`'s own endpoint list even though a manual `katana` invocation with identical flags found it every time (raised to 8 MiB, plus a new warning on `scanner.Err()` so a future overflow is loud, not silent). Live-verified end-to-end against the real crAPI container: `hackerfive recon --recon-depth full` now surfaces `js-static-joined http://.../workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report?report_id=` as a single, unambiguous candidate. `tests/eval/orchestrator_run.go`'s crAPI scenario was also switched from `Depth: "active"` to `Depth: "full"` — Wave 3 (crawl + JS-static analysis, where this whole fix lives) never runs below `"full"`, so the scenario couldn't have exercised this fix at all otherwise; every other `OrchestratorScenario` stays at `"active"` since none of their expected findings need Wave 3. **The candidate-generation gap itself is closed and confirmed**: with `Depth: "full"`, one orchestrator eval run did dispatch a real `scan.leaf` against the recon-discovered endpoint and produced 5 genuine `idor/high` findings (`report_id=1..5`, "otherToken retrieved real user data ... does not match the established denied-access baseline"). The eval scenario still doesn't pass end-to-end, though, for a separate, newly-discovered reason — see LT-166.
- **LT-165 (item 1 ✅ fixed 2026-09-19, item 2 deliberately not attempted — 2026-09-20 measurements in LT-172/LT-173 now supply the missing eval data: `NextAction` has no `max_tokens`/reasoning-effort cap, and 14 of 17 vAPI/DVWA turns were single-template dispatches that didn't need a model call)** — `pkg/llmfallback.Client.NextAction` calls against `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` (via OpenRouter) are slow in practice: 60-150s+ per call, live-observed during LT-162/163's re-verification. This is what made `Config.MinIterations`'s forced turn floor (LT-162's own fix) push crAPI's orchestrator run past a 10-minute budget (LT-163 raised the eval harness's timeout to compensate, but the underlying latency itself was unaddressed). Two independent angles were named:
  1. ✅ fixed — `buildOrchestratePrompt` (`pkg/llmfallback/orchestrate.go`) already capped turn *history* at `maxHistoryTurnsInPrompt` (LT-162's fix) but rendered the full plan-tree leaf list uncapped on every call — on a target with many leaves (crAPI's tree is larger than vAPI/DVWA/Juice Shop's), this was a real, still-uncapped contributor to prompt size (the tool catalog itself is a fixed ~6-entry list, negligible either way, so only the leaf list needed a cap). New `boundedLeavesForPrompt` caps the rendered leaf list at `maxLeafNodesInPrompt` (80), keeping every actionable (`StatusPending`/`StatusUnresolved`) leaf visible first — those are the only ones a `node_id` could usefully target this turn — and dropping an already-settled leaf (done/vetoed/escalated) first when the cap is hit; if even the actionable set alone exceeds the cap, it's truncated too (an omitted actionable leaf becomes visible on a later call once earlier ones resolve), rather than growing the prompt further. Tests: `TestBoundedLeavesForPrompt_UnderCap_ReturnsAllUnomitted`, `TestBoundedLeavesForPrompt_OverCap_KeepsActionableDropsSettledFirst`, `TestBoundedLeavesForPrompt_ActionableAloneExceedsCap`, `TestBuildOrchestratePrompt_CapsLeafListAndNotesOmission` (`pkg/llmfallback/orchestrate_test.go`).
  2. **Deliberately not attempted** — skipping `NextAction` outright when the tree has exactly one viable leaf was considered but not implemented: the catalog always also offers `recon.refresh`/`triage.rank`/`stop` alongside `scan.leaf`, so "one viable leaf" doesn't actually mean "one viable action" — forcing `scan.leaf` on that one leaf would remove the model's legitimate choice to triage already-collected findings, refresh recon first, or stop (e.g. a leaf that already escalated through its attempt/spend ceiling shouldn't be force-dispatched again). This is a real latency lever but a behavior-risking one without live eval data to confirm it doesn't regress recall — left for a future pass with an eval run to validate against, not guessed at here.
- **LT-166 (open — real root cause found and precisely diagnosed 2026-09-20, not yet fixed; re-confirmed later that day, and shown to corrupt the model's reasoning, not just cost time — see the 2026-09-20 "reasoning verification" section, LT-171)** — live-verified 2026-09-19, immediately after LT-164's fix: with `Depth: "full"`, the crAPI orchestrator eval run dispatched `scan.leaf` against `.../workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report?report_id={{id}}` and got 5 real `idor/high` findings — but the *identical* dispatch (same URL, same `report_id=1..5`, same "5 raw finding(s)" summary) then repeated two more times later in the same run, each a full ~1m20s re-scan, before the run exhausted the harness's 20-minute timeout and was killed. The 2026-09-19 pass ruled out two candidate causes by code reading (no live repro available that day): (1) a stale/re-dispatched leaf ID — `dispatchScanLeaf`'s guard, `planexec.RunPlan`'s unconditional `StatusDone` patch, and `PlanTree.Find`/`ApplyLeafUpdate`'s shared-mutex consistency form a provably-consistent chain, ruling this out; (2) `idorCandidatesAndSeeds`' own dedup (fixed as a real, separate, related bug that same day — see below) was confirmed not to be the mechanism either, since it dedups before `registry.Resolve`'s fan-out ever runs. A `warnDuplicateLeafTargets` diagnostic was added to catch "two leaves with an identical Detector+Target+EndpointTemplate at tree-build time," on the theory that some other candidate-generation path was producing true duplicates.

  **2026-09-20, with live crAPI access restored: the real mechanism was found, and it is not tree-level duplication at all.** A full re-run reproduced the exact symptom at scale — 13 separate `idor` leaves, each with a genuinely distinct, correctly-assigned `EndpointTemplate` (`/identity/api/auth/login?report_id={{id}}`, `/workshop/api/mechanic/download_report?report_id={{id}}`, etc. — confirmed directly from the run's own JSON `Result.Tree`, not inferred from the model's own turn-by-turn narration), yet **every single one of the 13 dispatches' real HTTP findings targeted the literal same 5 URLs** (`.../workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report?report_id=1..5`). `warnDuplicateLeafTargets` correctly did **not** fire — there is no tree-level duplication; each leaf really is distinct. Two synthetic reproductions were built to isolate where the divergence happens: one driving `pkg/scanner.Engine` directly with two different `EndpointTemplate` values in the same process, and one driving `pkg/orchestrator`'s own `dispatch()` against a two-leaf tree — **both came back clean**, proving `pkg/orchestrator`/`pkg/planexec`'s config plumbing and `pkg/scanner`/`pkg/detectors/idor`'s request-building are correct in isolation. The difference in both cases: neither synthetic repro had `--templates` set.

  **Root cause: `templates/idor/crapi-mechanic-report.yaml`** — a real, pre-existing, `idor`-tagged native template hardcoded to `{{BaseURL}}/workshop/api/mechanic/mechanic_report?report_id={{RangeInt(1|100)}}` (see [20-setup-testing-targets.md](20-setup-testing-targets.md)'s crAPI section; this template is genuinely correct and does real, valuable detection work in `hackerfive scan`). `pkg/planexec.RunPlan` has an LT-18 part (c) optimization: only the *first* leaf dispatched per host carries the additive full-template-corpus load (`corpusLeaves`, keyed by `seenHost` within one `RunPlan` call) — every other leaf on that host runs its own detector only, since re-loading/re-running the whole tag-scoped corpus per leaf would be needlessly expensive. That bookkeeping is scoped to a single `RunPlan` call, which is exactly right for `hackerfive scan`/webui Plan Preview (one `RunPlan` call dispatches every leaf together) — but `pkg/orchestrator`'s `dispatchScanLeaf` calls `RunPlan` **once per leaf**, each with every other leaf marked `Excluded`. From inside any one of those calls, the single non-excluded leaf always looks like "the first (and only) leaf on this host" — so **every one of the 13 `scan.leaf` dispatches independently reloaded and re-fired the full tag-scoped template corpus**, including `crapi-mechanic-report.yaml`, regardless of which leaf's own `EndpointTemplate` the model thought it was testing. The 65 total findings across the run are genuine (crAPI really is vulnerable at `mechanic_report`), but 12 of the 13 "leaf confirmations" are the same template re-firing on an unrelated leaf's turn, not that leaf's own idor-detector run against its own distinct endpoint (which most likely ran too and correctly found nothing, silently outnumbered by the template hits).

  **Not fixed this round** — needs a design decision on where "has the additive corpus pass already run for host X, this whole orchestrator run" is tracked, since `RunPlan`'s own per-call `corpusLeaves` map has no visibility across `dispatchScanLeaf`'s separate calls (candidates: thread a per-host-already-loaded set through `orchestrator.Config`/the `PlanTree` itself and pass it into a new `ExecOptions` field, vs. giving the orchestrator its own one-time-per-host corpus pass separate from `RunPlan`'s). **Practical implication for this session's already-committed eval table**: crAPI's "13/22 (isolated)" row and its "5 unexpected FPs" are not wrong about the fixture-prefix win (idor- and nuclei-http-missing-security-headers are both genuinely present), but the raw finding volume/per-leaf attribution behind that row is inflated by this bug, not a sign of the orchestrator genuinely testing 12 more real candidate endpoints. Left as an honest caveat here rather than silently correcting the already-committed table.

  Superseded by this finding but kept for history: `warnDuplicateLeafTargets` (`pkg/orchestrator/orchestrator.go`, unit-tested) remains a real, correct diagnostic for actual tree-level duplication — it simply isn't what LT-166 turned out to be.

  **2026-09-23 — real-scope evidence (LT-183 j) shows this is no longer a wasted-turns nuisance, it is a hard scale blocker.** `TestAblationRealScope` (the new LT-183(j) harness) against `*.nettix.com.pe` with a `no-model` arm — zero model turns, so LT-171's model-facing symptom is out of the picture entirely — still could not get past a handful of hosts in 90 minutes: every single `scan.leaf` dispatch took roughly 57-62 seconds, *even leaves whose own tag-filtered template count was 1* (`loaded 1 nuclei-compatible, 0 native templates (0 rejected, 0 filtered by tag)`, no "via the LT-106 parse cache" line, so `cacheHitDirs == 0` on every one of them). Directly curling the same hosts (`mail.nettix.com.pe`, `ixn.nettix.com.pe`, `ns1.nettix.com.pe`) returned in 0.1-0.4s each, ruling out target-side slowness. This is exactly the mechanism this entry already diagnosed 2026-09-20 (`dispatchScanLeaf` calling `RunPlan` once per leaf, so the additive full-corpus load's `seenHost` bookkeeping never accumulates across leaves within one orchestrator run) — but crAPI's own tree only had ~13-25 leaves, so the cost read as "some wasted re-fires." At a real host count (`*.nettix.com.pe` recon found ~20+ live hosts), the same per-leaf tax multiplies by every leaf on every host, and a `no-model` run — the cheapest, fastest arm that exists — cannot finish within any timeout this harness can practically set. **This is now the actual blocker on LT-183(j)**, not leaf count or model behavior: no arm comparison at real scope is measurable until this is fixed, because none of them can finish. Elevated from "not fixed this round" to blocking.
