  **Fixed 2026-09-23**, closing the "Not fixed this round" design question above. `planexec.ExecOptions` gained `CorpusHostState map[string]bool` — optional, nil-safe. `RunPlan`'s existing LT-18-part-(c) `seenHost` bookkeeping now reads/writes this caller-supplied map (a reference type) instead of always allocating a fresh one; every caller that leaves it nil (`hackerfive scan`, webui, MCP — everyone but the orchestrator) is unaffected, since a fresh map each call is exactly what those single-RunPlan-call callers already had. `orchestrator.Config` gained an unexported `corpusHostState map[string]bool`, allocated once per `Run` call (same "set by Run, not part of the public surface" convention as the existing `observeRecon` field) and passed into every `dispatchScanLeaf` → `runScanLeafOnce` → `RunPlan` call's `ExecOptions.CorpusHostState` — since `Config` is passed by value down the call chain but a map is a reference type, every copy shares the one allocation for the life of one agent run. New regression tests (`pkg/planexec/executor_test.go`): `TestRunPlan_CorpusHostState_PersistsAcrossSeparateCalls` reproduces `dispatchScanLeaf`'s exact shape (three single-leaf RunPlan calls, same host, sharing one map) and asserts only the first loads the corpus; `TestRunPlan_CorpusHostState_NilLeavesEachCallIndependent` pins the unchanged nil-default behavior. `go test ./... -race` clean (the pre-existing, unrelated `pkg/scriptexec` LT-181 environment failure aside).

  **Live-reverified against `*.nettix.com.pe` (a separate LT-183(j) real-scope measurement branch): the fix is correct but insufficient by itself for that target** — see LT-197. Most of nettix's per-leaf cost turned out to be a different, second mechanism (many tech-fingerprint-driven specific-template leaves per host, each paying a full corpus walk via `LoadDirByIDs`, which this fix does not touch since a specific-template leaf was never routed through `seenHost` — see RunPlan's own "a specific-template leaf always keeps the corpus" design, unchanged and correct on its own terms). The LT-166 mechanism itself (redundant reload for `misconfig`/`idor`/etc.-type leaves) is fixed and proven via the regression test above; LT-197 is what still blocks a full real-scope LT-183(j) run.
  **A real, previously-unfound instance of "a different leaf ID resolving to an identical dispatch" was found and fixed 2026-09-19** (via a fresh static-code-reading pass, not a live crAPI re-run — no Docker/API key available in this environment; the exact crAPI trigger therefore remains unconfirmed, and this fix is reported honestly as *a* closed gap in the dedup chain, not *the* proven root cause). `idorCandidatesAndSeeds`'s two candidate sources formatted the *same* (path, key) two different ways: the per-endpoint `idShapedQueryCandidate` pass (an ID-*named* key, e.g. `report_id`) preserves every sibling query param in its template (`"?report_id={{id}}&foo=bar"`), while the separate LT-83 `numericQueryIDCandidates` pass (an unnamed-but-varying numeric key) drops all sibling params (`"?report_id={{id}}"`) — for a key whose name happens to also look ID-shaped *and* whose value varies across ≥2 observations, both passes fire and produce two different template strings for what is really one identifier position, defeating `seen[tmpl]`'s exact-string dedup and fanning out two `idor` leaves (LT-91) that dispatch an effectively identical check — precisely the class of bug LT-166 hypothesized, just located in candidate generation rather than in `Resolve`'s fan-out itself. **Fixed:** `numericQueryIDCandidates` (`pkg/recon/suggest.go`) now returns a `queryIDKey{path, key}` struct instead of a pre-formatted string; `idShapedCandidate`/`idShapedQueryCandidate` now also return the matched query key; `idorCandidatesAndSeeds` records each per-endpoint query match's `(path, key)` in a `seenQueryKey` set and skips a `numericQueryIDCandidates` result already covered by it, before ever formatting/comparing template strings. `SuggestSQLiTargets`'s own `numericQueryIDCandidates` caller was updated to use the new struct fields directly instead of parsing the old formatted-string return (a simplification, not a behavior change — it already merged both signals' params into one path-keyed target, so it was never exposed to this bug the way `idorCandidatesAndSeeds`' per-leaf fan-out was). Test: `TestSuggestIDOREndpointCandidates`'s new "an id-named query key varying across >= 2 observations must not also fan out via the LT-83 numeric path" case (`pkg/recon/suggest_test.go`) reproduces the exact two-source collision and asserts exactly one, sibling-preserving candidate survives.
- **LT-167 ✅ fixed 2026-09-20 — real gap, live-discovered the same day** — `pkg/scanner/scope.Scope.Allowed` (`scope.go:120-151`) matched by hostname only: it parsed `u.Hostname()` from both the scope entry and the target, discarding the port entirely, so a scope file containing a bare `localhost` (the exact convention `tests/eval`'s own `hostOnly()` helper and this session's manual `--scope` files both used) allowed a request to **any port** on that host, not just the one the operator actually pointed the run at. Live-discovered while re-verifying LT-162: with crAPI (port 8888), DVWA (port 80), Juice Shop (port 3000), and vAPI (port 8000) all running simultaneously for convenience, a manual `hackerfive agent -t http://localhost:8888 --scope <file with "localhost">` run had its `recon.refresh` action redirected entirely onto `http://localhost` (port 80, i.e. DVWA) — the run produced 35 real findings, every one of them DVWA's, zero of them crAPI's. Two design options were weighed: (a) grow `Scope`'s domain-entry format with an optional `:port` suffix, or (b) have `dispatchReconRefresh` validate a fresh `target` against the plan tree's own already-established host:port set instead of deferring to the coarser file-level `Scope`. Option (b) turned out to be far more expensive than it looked: `pkg/registry`'s whole `PlanNode.Target` convention is hostname-only end to end (`newEndpointLeaf`, every host node built in `decisionengine.go`) — the actual scheme+port is reconstructed only at dispatch time, from the original `recon.ReconResult`'s endpoints (`reconHostBaseURL`), never stored on the tree itself. Threading port-awareness through the tree just for this one guard would have meant touching dozens of leaf-construction sites for no other benefit. **Fixed via (a) instead**: `scope.Scope`'s domain entries (`domainEntry{pattern, port}`) now accept an optional trailing `:port` (parsed via `net.SplitHostPort`, so a bare unbracketed IPv6 entry is correctly left alone rather than misparsed); `Allowed` compares it against the target's own port (defaulted to 80/443 by scheme when the target omits one) only when the entry specifies one — an entry with no port keeps matching any port, so every scope file written before this fix keeps working unmodified. `dispatchReconRefresh` itself needed no change: `recon.Run`'s existing top-of-function `Scope.Allowed` check (`recon.go:445`) now genuinely catches the drift once the scope file pins a port. `tests/eval`'s `hostOnly()` helper (renamed `hostScopeEntry`, used by both `agent_eval_test.go` and `orchestrator_eval_test.go`) now writes `host:port` instead of a bare host, closing the exact reproduction vehicle this bug was found through. New tests: `TestAllowed_PortPinnedEntry_RejectsOtherPortsOnSameHost`, `TestAllowed_PortlessEntry_StillMatchesAnyPort`, `TestAllowed_PortPinnedWildcardEntry`, `TestEntries_PortPinnedEntry_RoundTrips` (`pkg/scanner/scope/scope_test.go`). Note this is opt-in: an operator's existing bare-hostname scope entry is unaffected until they actually add `:port` — the fix gives `--scope` the vocabulary to express a port boundary, but doesn't retroactively tighten a scope file nobody has edited.
